// Robix language syntax reference
// Revised design incorporating the improvement notes throughout the original file.
// This is an illustrative language specification and example module.
// Features marked as proposed describe intended Robix behavior and require compiler support.

// The language will have both compiler and interpreter called robix and robixc.
// The language is always strict. The compiler and the interpreter both enforce type safety and only accept a module when every type resolves.
// The language must have same performance as C and C++ and must provide same control over hardware while easy like below syntax.
// Windows comes later.
// Add clear examples at Examples folder for every possible use of that feature or syntax which is implemented with real examples.
The lagnuage will can have both entry point from procedural with private int main and also from class procedural one will be more dominant. Will give error both by interpreter and compiler if two are defined at once in one file.

// Their must be Filename.test.rx file for each and every file which will have tests for each function in that file and if this file not exists or tests not exists then interpreter will ignore while the compiler will give error.


// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 3. Type system, conversions, precision, and user-defined types
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

// Arbitrary-width floating types, such as Float<128>, are still open.
Float<128> wideFloat = Float<128>.From(1.0);

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 4. Composite types, collections, tuples, and universal values
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

// Generic constraints and user-defined generic types.
Struct RoboResult<TValue, TError> {
    Public Bool IsSuccess;
    Public TValue? Value;
    Public TError? Error;
}

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 6. Functions, arguments, documentation, and return values
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

Public Function RoboUpdate(Mut Int speed, Ref<Mut String> name) -> Void
Explain {
    Description: "Update a speed and a referenced name.";
    Input: speed = "Mutable speed", name = "Mutable name reference";
    Processing: "Increase speed and replace the name.";
    Output: "No return value.";
} {
    speed = speed + 10;
    name.Value = "RoboRunner";
}

// Async, Promise/Task, timers, cancellation, and scheduling.
// Robix uses Task<T> as its standard asynchronous operation type. Promise<T>
// is a compatible promise-style name for APIs that expose resolve/reject.
// Async functions can call other async functions directly. Calling an async
// function starts/returns its operation; Await observes its eventual result.
// Await is only valid inside Async functions (or an async top-level context).

Public Async Function RoboReadSensor() -> Task<Int> {
    Await Task.Delay(Duration.Milliseconds(50));
    Return Hardware.ReadSensor();
}

Public Async Function RoboCalculateReading() -> Task<Float> {
    // No special wrapper is needed to call another async function.
    Int raw = Await RoboReadSensor();
    Float calibrated = Await RoboCalibrate(raw);
    Return calibrated;
}

Public Async Function RoboCalibrate(Int raw) -> Task<Float> {
    Await Task.Yield();
    Return raw.ToFloat() * 1.25;
}

// Async functions may call one another at any depth. The compiler checks
// awaited return types and propagates failures through the Task chain.
Public Async Function RoboRunWorkflow() -> Task<String> {
    Int reading = Await RoboReadSensor();
    Float result = Await RoboCalibrate(reading);
    Return $"Calibrated reading: {result}";
}

// A Promise is a one-time value that will be fulfilled or rejected later.
// Task<T> and Promise<T> interoperate; APIs may choose either spelling.
Let promiseValue: Promise<Int> = Promise<Int>.Create(
    (resolve, reject) => {
        resolve(42);
    }
);

Public Async Function RoboUsePromise() -> Task<Int> {
    Int value = Await promiseValue;
    Return value;
}

// Create a promise from callback-based APIs.
Let callbackPromise = Promise<String>.FromCallback(
    (resolve, reject) => LegacyApi.ReadText(
        onSuccess: text => resolve(text),
        onError: error => reject(error)
    )
);

// Resolve/reject explicitly, or use the concise completed/faulted factories.
Let completed = Promise<Int>.Resolved(10);
Let failed = Promise<Int>.Rejected("Sensor unavailable");

// Promise/Task composition.
Public Async Function RoboComposeTasks() -> Task<Int> {
    Int first = Await Task.FromResult(10);
    Int second = Await Task.Run(() => RoboWorker(5));
    Return first + second;
}

// Start work concurrently, then await the operations together.
Public Async Function RoboParallelWork() -> Task<(String, Int)> {
    Task<String> nameTask = Network.SendText("robot-name");
    Task<Int> sensorTask = RoboReadSensor();

    (String name, Int reading) = Await Task.WhenAll(nameTask, sensorTask);
    Return (name, reading);
}

// Promise combinators, corresponding to common JavaScript Promise helpers.
Public Async Function RoboPromiseCombinators() -> Task<Void> {
    Array<Task<Int>> jobs = [
        Task.Run(() => 10),
        Task.Run(() => 20),
        Task.Run(() => 30)
    ];

    Array<Int> allValues = Await Promise.All(jobs);
    Int firstSettled = Await Promise.Race(jobs);
    Int firstSuccessful = Await Promise.Any(jobs);
    Array<PromiseSettlement<Int>> outcomes = Await Promise.AllSettled(jobs);

    Print(allValues);
    Print(firstSettled);
    Print(firstSuccessful);
    Print(outcomes);
}

// Timeout: fail or return a fallback if an operation takes too long.
// Timeout does not necessarily stop the underlying operation unless a
// cancellation token is also supplied.
Public Async Function RoboDownloadWithTimeout() -> Task<String> {
    Return Await Network.SendText("ping").WithTimeout(
        Duration.Seconds(5),
        onTimeout: TimeoutError("Download took too long.")
    );
}

Public Async Function RoboDownloadWithFallback() -> Task<String> {
    Return Await Network.SendText("ping").WithTimeout(
        Duration.Seconds(3),
        fallback: "offline"
    );
}

// Cancellation is cooperative. Pass a token to operations that support it.
Public Async Function RoboCancelableWork(CancellationToken token) -> Task<Int> {
    For index In 0..100 {
        token.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
        Await Task.Delay(Duration.Milliseconds(20), token);
        ProcessStep(index);
    }
    Return 100;
}

Public Async Function RoboCancelExample() -> Task<Void> {
    CancellationSource source = CancellationSource();
    Task<Int> work = RoboCancelableWork(source.Token);

    Await Task.Delay(Duration.Seconds(1));
    source.Cancel("User requested cancellation.");

    Try {
        Int result = Await work;
        Print(result);
    } Catch (OperationCanceledError error) {
        Print(error.Message);
    }
}

// One-shot timeout callbacks. The returned handle can cancel the callback.
TimerHandle timeoutHandle = Timer.SetTimeout(
    () => Print("Runs once after one second."),
    Duration.Seconds(1)
);
timeoutHandle.Cancel();

// Interval callbacks repeat until cancelled. Intervals should not overlap by
// default: if a callback takes longer than the interval, the next tick waits.
TimerHandle intervalHandle = Timer.SetInterval(
    () => Print("Repeats every 500 ms."),
    Duration.Milliseconds(500)
);
Timer.ClearInterval(intervalHandle);

// Async timeout/interval callbacks are supported as well.
TimerHandle asyncInterval = Timer.SetIntervalAsync(
    Async Function() {
        Int reading = Await RoboReadSensor();
        Print(reading);
    },
    Duration.Seconds(2),
    overlap: False
);
Timer.Clear(asyncInterval);

// Promise-based delay and scheduling helpers.
Await Task.Delay(Duration.Seconds(1));
Await Timer.Sleep(Duration.Milliseconds(250));
Task.Yield();                         // Continue on a later scheduler turn.
Scheduler.QueueMicrotask(() => Print("Microtask"));
Scheduler.Queue(() => Print("Scheduled task"));

// Debounce runs after calls stop arriving for the specified duration.
// Throttle allows at most one execution per time window.
Let saveDebounced = Function.Debounce(
    (String value) => SaveDraft(value),
    Duration.Milliseconds(300)
);
Let updateThrottled = Function.Throttle(
    () => RefreshDashboard(),
    Duration.Seconds(1)
);

// Polling helper: stop when the predicate succeeds or timeout expires.
Public Async Function RoboWaitForConnection() -> Task<Bool> {
    Return Await Async.WaitUntil(
        () => Network.IsConnected(),
        interval: Duration.Milliseconds(100),
        timeout: Duration.Seconds(10)
    );
}

// Async iteration and streams support backpressure and cancellation.
Public Async Function RoboWatchSensors(CancellationToken token) -> Task<Void> {
    AsyncStream<Int> readings = Hardware.WatchSensor(token);
    Await For reading In readings {
        Print(reading);
    }
}

// Async locks and synchronization primitives.
Public Async Function RoboSafeUpdate(AsyncLock gate) -> Task<Void> {
    Using lease = Await gate.AcquireAsync();
    UpdateSharedState();
}

// Error handling: rejected promises/tasks are raised at Await and can be
// handled with Try/Catch or converted to Result values.
Public Async Function RoboTryNetwork() -> Task<Result<String, NetworkError>> {
    Try {
        String response = Await Network.SendText("ping");
        Return Success(response);
    } Catch (NetworkError error) {
        Return Failure(error);
    }
}

// Top-level Await is allowed in executable modules. Library modules should
// expose async functions rather than performing work during import.
Await RoboRunWorkflow();

// Async API behavior rules:
// - Async Function always returns Task<T> (or Task<Void>), even when its
//   declared result is T. Await obtains the T value.
// - Async functions may call other async functions directly and Await them;
//   no callback conversion or extra Promise constructor is required.
// - Calling without Await starts/retains the asynchronous operation. A
//   compiler warning is emitted for an unobserved Task unless explicitly
//   detached with Task.Detach(...).
// - Await propagates exceptions/rejections. Try/Catch handles them normally.
// - Task.WhenAll/Promise.All waits for all operations; Race returns the first
//   settled result; Any returns the first fulfilled result; AllSettled returns
//   every outcome without failing fast.
// - Timeout and cancellation are separate: timeout bounds waiting, while
//   cancellation asks the underlying operation to stop cooperatively.
// - Timer callbacks run on the scheduler, not on a guaranteed new thread.
// - Interval callbacks default to non-overlapping execution; callers may opt
//   into overlap explicitly when that is safe.
// - Timers return cancellable handles. Timer handles should be disposed or
//   cancelled when no longer needed.
// - Async streams provide backpressure; each next item is requested as the
//   consumer advances.
// - The runtime defines task scheduling, microtask ordering, timer precision,
//   exception propagation, and synchronization-context behavior consistently.

// Async, iterator, generator, and compile-time functions.
Public Async Function RoboDownload() -> Task<String>
Explain {
    Description: "Download a text response.";
    Input: None;
    Processing: "Send a network request asynchronously.";
    Output: "Downloaded text.";
} {
    String data = Await Network.SendText("ping");
    Return data;
}

Public Iterator Function RoboCount(Int start, Int end) -> Int {
    For index In start..=end {
        Yield index;
    }
}

// Anonymous functions and lambdas.
Let noNameTask = Task.Run(Function() => RoboWorker(1));

// Partial application and function composition.
Let addTen = RoboAdd.Partial(10);
Int twenty = addTen(10);
Let transform = Function.Compose(square, Function(n) => n + 1);

// Function declarations may use any supported number of parameters. The
// compiler/runtime may enforce resource limits, but the language has no
// arbitrary small fixed argument-count limit.

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 7. Classes, structs, interfaces, traits, enums, and services
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

Public Abstract Class RoboRobot {
    Protected String robotName;
    Protected Int batteryLevel;

    Public Abstract Function Move() -> Void;

    Public Function GetBatteryLevel() -> Int {
        Return batteryLevel;
    }
}

// Simple Constructor and Destructor keywords.
Public Class RoboWalkingRobot : RoboRobot, RoboDrivable {
    Private String serialNumber;

    Public Constructor(String name, String serial = "UNASSIGNED") {
        Base.Constructor();
        robotName = name;
        serialNumber = serial;
        batteryLevel = 100;
    }

    Public Override Function Move() -> Void {
        Print("Walking forward");
    }

    Public Function GetSerialNumber() -> String {
        Return serialNumber;
    }

    Public Destructor() {
        // Release owned resources. Destructors should be deterministic and safe.
    }
}

// Traditional constructor-name syntax may also be supported as shorthand.
Public Class RoboFlyingRobot : RoboRobot {
    Public Constructor(String name = "RoboFly") {
        Base.Constructor();
        robotName = name;
        batteryLevel = 100;
    }

    Public Override Function Move() -> Void {
        Print("Flying forward");
    }
}

// Base constructor/destructor calls are explicit and ordered.
Public Class RoboAdvancedRobot : RoboWalkingRobot {
    Public Constructor(String name) : Base.Constructor(name) {
        Print("Advanced robot initialized");
    }

    Public Destructor() {
        Print("Advanced robot cleanup");
        Base.Destructor();
    }
}

// Multiple inheritance uses explicit base initialization. Name conflicts must
// be resolved with BaseName.Member or an Override declaration.
Public Class RoboHybridRobot : RoboWalkingRobot, RoboFlyingCapability {
    Public Constructor(String name)
        : Base<RoboWalkingRobot>.Constructor(name)
        : Base<RoboFlyingCapability>.Constructor() {
        robotName = name;
    }

    Public Override Function Move() -> Void {
        Print("Walking and flying");
    }
}

Public Struct RoboPoint {
    Public Float X;
    Public Float Y;

    Public Constructor(Float x, Float y) {
        X = x;
        Y = y;
    }
}

Public Interface RoboDrivable {
    Public Function Move() -> Void;
}

Public Interface RoboComparable<T> {
    Public Function CompareTo(T other) -> Int;
}

Public Trait RoboIterable<T> {
    Public Function GetIterator() -> Iterator<T>;
}

Public Trait RoboCloneable<T> {
    Public Function Clone() -> T;
}

Public Enum RoboState {
    Idle,
    Moving,
    Charging,
    Error(Int errorCode)
}

Public Service RoboStatusService {
    Public Function GetStatus(Int id) -> Int;
}

Implement RoboStatusService {
    Public Function GetStatus(Int id) -> Int {
        Return id;
    }
}

Connect RoboStatusService;

// Visibility rules:
// Public: accessible everywhere permitted.
// Protected: accessible in the declaring type and derived types.
// Private: accessible only inside the declaring type.
// Internal: accessible inside the same package/module.
// Inheritance supports abstract, virtual, override, sealed/final, and explicit
// base calls. Composition is preferred where multiple inheritance is ambiguous.

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

// Applications may be launched with console controls, for example:
// robix run RobotApp --console=off
// robix run RobotApp --console=on --port=8080
// The runtime owns console creation; applications can inspect Runtime.Console.Enabled.

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 9. Exceptions, defer, using, unsafe, assembly, and labels
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

Try {
    Throw RoboException("Sensor failure");
} Catch (RoboException error) {
    Print(error.Message);
} Finally {
    Print("Cleanup complete");
}

Defer {
    Print("Runs when the current scope exits");
}

Using file = Files.Open("robot.log") {
    file.Read();
}

Unsafe {
    Int sensorValue = 100;
    Pointer<Int> sensorPointer = Pointer.Of(sensorValue);
    *sensorPointer = 250;
}

ASM("NOP");
ASM("movq $1, $0", Out: y, In: x);
ASM("outb $0, $1", In: value, In: port, Clobber: "memory");

Goto RoboLabel;
RoboLabel:
Print("jump target");

// Goto is restricted to legal control-flow regions and cannot jump into scopes
// that would bypass initialization, cleanup, or resource-safety rules.

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 10. Memory, pointers, references, and concurrency
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

Unsafe {
    Pointer<Byte> memory = Memory.Allocate<Byte>(1024);
    *memory = 42;
    Pointer<Byte> next = memory + 8;
    Memory.Copy(next, memory, 128);
    Memory.Free(memory);
}

Unsafe {
    Volatile.Store(targetPointer, 42);
    Int loaded = Volatile.Load(sourcePointer);
}

Lock sensorLock {
    UpdateSharedSensorState();
}

Atomic<Int> sharedCounter = Atomic<Int>(0);
sharedCounter.Increment();

Task task = Task.Run(Function() => RoboWorker(1));
Await task;

    // Compile-time macro expansion example.
    body;
    body;

// Reflection and dynamic invocation.
TypeInfo controllerType = Reflect.TypeOf(RoboController);
Any instance = Reflect.Create(controllerType);
Reflect.Invoke(instance, "Start");

// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 19. Language design rules summary
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

// 1. Any stores values of any type. Dynamic enables runtime member binding.
// 2. Type inference chooses a suitable type from the initializer.
// 3. Conversions are available as value methods and type constructors.
// 4. Numeric widths and floating precision can be selected explicitly.
// 5. Multi-declaration, tuple assignment, and chained assignment are supported.
// 6. Functions support defaults, named arguments, generics, variadic arguments,
//    multiple returns, async behavior, iterators, and anonymous expressions.
// 7. Operators have callable function forms. Custom operators declare
//    precedence/associativity and can be implemented for user-defined types.
// 8. OOP supports access modifiers, constructors, destructors, base calls,
//    abstract/virtual/override/final members, and controlled multiple inheritance.
// 9. Main receives command-line arguments and returns a standard ExitCode or
//    ExecutionResult. Raw integer exit values remain a warned compatibility path.
// 10. Standard library APIs should have consistent naming, documentation,
//     error handling, and discoverable module organization.
// 11. Async Function returns Task<T>; Await unwraps its result. Async functions
//     compose directly with other async functions.
// 12. Promise<T> interoperates with Task<T> and supports resolve/reject,
//     chaining, and All/Race/Any/AllSettled combinators.
// 13. Timer supports one-shot timeouts, repeating intervals, async callbacks,
//     cancellation handles, delay, debounce, throttle, and polling.
// 14. Cancellation is cooperative through CancellationToken; timeout and
//     cancellation remain distinct concepts.
// 15. Async streams, async locks, scheduler queues, and top-level Await are
//     supported with defined runtime scheduling and error semantics.
// 16. Strict checking covers variables, inference, functions, generics, nullability,
//     numeric conversions, collections, OOP, operators, async code, errors,
//     FFI boundaries, and control-flow exhaustiveness.
//
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
// 20. Mandatory type-safe programming
// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

// TYPE SAFETY
// -----------

// 20.1 Variable declarations and inference
// ----------------------------------------

// Every variable has one statically known type in Strict mode.
Let readings: Array<Float> = [1.2, 2.4, 3.6];

// An uninitialized variable MUST declare its type and MUST be assigned before
// its first read. Definite-assignment analysis checks every control-flow path.
Var nextReading: Float;
nextReading = 4.2;

// Var does not mean dynamically typed. Its type is fixed after inference.
// Use a declared union or Any when multiple value types are genuinely needed.

// 20.2 Any, Dynamic, unions, and safe narrowing
// ---------------------------------------------

// Any can store a value of any Robix type, but it does not permit arbitrary
// operations. A value must be narrowed or checked before type-specific use.
Var payload: Any = 42;

Match payload {
    Int number => Print(number + 1),
    String text => Print(text.Length()),
    Bool flag => Print(flag),
    _ => Print("Unsupported payload type")
}

// Dynamic performs runtime member lookup. It is permitted in interpreter
// Runtime mode, but is prohibited in Strict compiled code unless isolated
// behind an explicitly declared unsafe/dynamic boundary.
// Dynamic externalValue;          // compile-time error in Strict mode

// Prefer a closed union when the set of possible types is known.
Type SensorValue = Int | Float | String;

Function DescribeSensor(SensorValue value) -> String {
    Match value {
        Int number => Return $"Integer: {number}",
        Float number => Return $"Float: {number}",
        String text => Return $"Text: {text}"
    }
}

// Union matches must be exhaustive, or include a fallback arm.
// The compiler narrows the value's type within each matching branch.

// 20.3 Function signatures and return types
// -----------------------------------------

// Every function in Strict mode must declare parameter types and a return type.
// No implicit Any parameters, untyped returns, or ambiguous overloads.
Public Function Add(Int left, Int right) -> Int {
    Return left + right;
}

Public Function FindRobot(String id) -> Option<Robot> {
    Return RobotDatabase.Find(id);
}

// Void means the function returns no value. Every non-Void path must return
// a value compatible with the declared return type.
Function IsBatteryLow(Int percent) -> Bool {
    Return percent < 20;
}

// Parameters are immutable by default. Mut and Ref must be explicit.
Function Increase(mut Int value, Int amount) -> Void {
    value = value + amount;
}

// Named/default arguments are checked against the declared parameter types.
Function MoveRobot(
    String robotId,
    Float distance = 1.0,
    Bool avoidObstacles = True
) -> Result<Void, MovementError> {
    Return Navigation.Move(robotId, distance, avoidObstacles);
}

// Variadic arguments are homogeneous unless explicitly declared as a tuple
// or union. The element type is checked at each call site.
Function SumAll(params Int[] values) -> Int {
    Int total = 0;
    For value In values {
        total = total + value;
    }
    Return total;
}

// Generic functions declare type parameters and constraints.
Function First<T>(Array<T> values) -> Option<T>
    Where T: Copy {
    If values.Count() == 0 {
        Return None();
    }
    Return Some(values[0]);
}

// Generic constraints are compile-time contracts. A call is rejected if its
// type argument does not satisfy every declared constraint.
Function Compare<T>(T left, T right) -> Int
    Where T: Comparable<T> {
    Return left.CompareTo(right);
}

// 20.4 Async and Promise type safety
// ----------------------------------

// Async functions declare Task<T> (or Promise<T>) as their asynchronous
// result. Await produces exactly T. The compiler checks every Await expression.
Public Async Function ReadBattery() -> Task<Int> {
    Return Await Hardware.ReadBatteryLevel();
}

Public Async Function GetBatteryLabel() -> Task<String> {
    Int level = Await ReadBattery();
    Return $"Battery: {level}%";
}

// Task<T> cannot be assigned to T without Await.
// Let level: Int = ReadBattery();    // compile-time error
// Let level: Int = Await ReadBattery(); // valid inside Async Function

// WhenAll preserves the types and order of its input operations.
Public Async Function ReadRobotState() -> Task<(Int, String)> {
    Task<Int> batteryTask = ReadBattery();
    Task<String> nameTask = RobotDatabase.GetNameAsync();

    (Int battery, String name) =
        Await Task.WhenAll(batteryTask, nameTask);

    Return (battery, name);
}

// Detached tasks must be explicit and must provide an error-observation policy.
Task.Detach(
    Background.FlushLogs(),
    onError: error => Log.Error(error)
);

// 20.5 Null safety and optional values
// ------------------------------------

// A non-nullable reference cannot receive null. Add ? to make it optional.
Let requiredName: String = "RoboBot";
Let optionalName: String? = null;

// The compiler requires a null check, null-coalescing expression, safe access,
// or explicit unwrap before using an optional value as non-null.
If optionalName != null {
    Print(optionalName.Length());
}

Let displayName = optionalName ?? "Unknown";
Let nameLength = optionalName?.Length() ?? 0;

// Explicit unwrap is permitted but may fail if the value is null.
// Prefer pattern matching or a fallback when failure is possible.
Let confirmedName = optionalName!;

// Option<T> is the explicit algebraic form of a value that may be absent.
// Option<T> and T? have defined, interoperable conversion rules.

// 20.6 Numeric safety and conversions
// -----------------------------------

// No silent narrowing, signedness changes, or precision-losing conversions
// are permitted in Strict mode. Use checked conversions or explicit policies.
Let wideNumber: Int64 = 9_000_000;
Let narrowNumber: Int = Int.FromChecked(wideNumber);

// Checked conversion returns Result<T, ConversionError> when it may fail.
Let parsedNumber: Result<Int, ParseError> = Int.TryParse("123");

// Explicit conversion makes the programmer's intent visible.
Let roundedReading: Int = sensorPrecision.ToInt(Rounding.Nearest);
Let exactDecimal: Decimal = Decimal.From("12.50", scale: 2);

// Lossy conversion requires a named policy or an explicitly marked cast.
// Let badNarrowing: UInt8 = 1000;    // compile-time range error
// Let badFloat: Int = 3.9;           // compile-time conversion error

// Integer overflow behavior is defined by type and build policy:
// Checked arithmetic reports OverflowError; wrapping arithmetic uses
// explicit operators/functions such as WrappingAdd; saturating arithmetic
// uses SaturatingAdd. Strict mode never silently changes policy.
Let safeSum = Int.AddChecked(20, 30);
Let wrappedSum = Int.WrappingAdd(Int.MaxValue, 1);
Let cappedSum = UInt8.SaturatingAdd(250, 20);

// 20.7 Collections, tuples, and records
// -------------------------------------

// Collection element types are fixed. Empty collections need an explicit
// element type because inference has no element from which to infer.
Let emptyNames: Array<String> = [];
Let motorSpeeds: List<Int> = [100, 120, 140];
Let coordinates: (Float, Float) = (10.5, 20.5);

Struct RobotPosition {
    Public X: Float;
    Public Y: Float;
}

Let position: RobotPosition = RobotPosition(X: 1.0, Y: 2.0);

// Dictionary keys and values have fixed types. Key types must satisfy the
// Hashable/Equatable requirements of the dictionary implementation.
Let robotById: Map<String, Robot> = Map<String, Robot>();

// 20.8 Classes, interfaces, traits, and inheritance
// --------------------------------------------------

// Fields, properties, constructors, and method signatures are type-checked.
// Overrides must be substitutable for the base declaration. Access modifiers
// do not change type rules.
Public Interface IRobot {
    Function Move(Float distance) -> Result<Void, MovementError>;
}

Public Class TypedRobot Implements IRobot {
    Private speed: Float;

    Public Constructor(Float initialSpeed) {
        This.speed = initialSpeed;
    }

    Public Function Move(Float distance) -> Result<Void, MovementError> {
        Return Navigation.MoveDistance(distance, This.speed);
    }
}

// Every implemented interface member must match its declared signature.
// Generic variance, where supported, is explicit and checked by the compiler.

// 20.9 Operators and overload resolution
// --------------------------------------

// Operator overloads have declared parameter and result types. They must
// obey the operator's required arity and cannot return an unrelated type.
Public Operator +(RobotPosition left, RobotPosition right)
    -> RobotPosition {
    Return RobotPosition(
        X: left.X + right.X,
        Y: left.Y + right.Y
    );
}

// Ambiguous overload resolution is a compile-time error. Implicit conversions
// may not be used to silently select an unsafe overload.

// 20.10 Pattern matching and control-flow analysis
// ------------------------------------------------

// Match expressions must be exhaustive and each arm must produce a compatible
// result type. Conditions must be Bool; there is no truthy/falsy coercion.
Let batteryState: String = Match batteryPercent {
    80..=100 => "Full",
    30..79 => "Normal",
    0..29 => "Low"
};

// Definite assignment, unreachable branches, and narrowed types are analyzed.
// A variable narrowed inside a branch is not assumed narrowed after the branch.
If payload Is Int {
    Int safePayload = payload;
    Print(safePayload);
}

// 20.11 Exceptions, errors, and result types
// ------------------------------------------

// Thrown exceptions have declared/categorized error types where the function
// contract specifies them. Recoverable failures should normally use Result<T,E>.
Function LoadConfiguration(String path)
    -> Result<Configuration, ConfigurationError> {
    Return Configuration.Read(path);
}

Match LoadConfiguration("robot.config") {
    Success(config) => Apply(config),
    Failure(error) => Log.Error(error)
}

// Catch clauses are type-checked. A catch-all handler must be explicit.
// Finally blocks cannot change the function's declared return type.

// 20.12 External APIs, reflection, and unsafe boundaries
// ------------------------------------------------------

// External/FFI declarations must specify parameter and return types, calling
// convention, ownership, and error behavior. Untrusted values are validated
// at the boundary before entering typed code.
Extern Function NativeReadSensor() -> Int
    CallingConvention C;

// Reflection returns TypeInfo/Any. A reflected value must be checked and
// narrowed before it can enter a statically typed operation.
Let reflected: Any = Reflect.Invoke(instance, "GetBatteryLevel");
If reflected Is Int {
    Let reflectedBattery: Int = reflected;
    Print(reflectedBattery);
}

// Unsafe code is an explicit boundary. The compiler still checks what it can;
// pointer validity, aliasing, lifetime, and memory safety become programmer
// responsibilities and may require runtime instrumentation.
Unsafe {
    Pointer<Byte> rawBuffer = Memory.Allocate(64);
    // Validate length, lifetime, alignment, and ownership before access.
    Memory.Free(rawBuffer);
}

// 20.13 Type-safe modules and public API boundaries
// -------------------------------------------------

// Public APIs must expose concrete types, documented error behavior, and
// stable generic constraints. Dynamic/Any values must not leak into a public
// Strict API unless the public signature explicitly declares Any.
// Module imports are checked against exported declarations and package versions.

// 20.14 Compiler enforcement and diagnostics
// -------------------------------------------

// Compilation and interpretation MUST fail if any of these conditions remain:
// - A variable has no inferable or declared static type.
// - A value is assigned to an incompatible type.
// - A function parameter or return type is missing or inconsistent.
// - A non-Void function can reach its end without returning a value.
// - A nullable value is used without a safe check or explicit unwrap.
// - A numeric conversion can narrow, overflow, or lose precision implicitly.
// - A union/enum/pattern match is not exhaustive where exhaustiveness is required.
// - A generic constraint is not satisfied.
// - An overload or operator call is ambiguous.
// - An async Task/Promise value is used as its inner value without Await.
// - An unhandled unsafe/dynamic boundary violates the project policy.
// - A public API violates its declared type or ownership contract.
//
// Warnings may be configured as errors. Type errors are never downgraded to
// warnings in Strict compilation. The compiler must identify the source
// location, expected type, actual type, and a useful correction where possible.
//
// The interpreter applies the same static checks before execution. It does
// not start a module that still has a type error.
//
// 20.15 Strict reference
// ---------------------

Let typedName: String = "RoboBot";
Var typedCount: Int = 10;
Let inferredValue = 25;                 // inferred Int
Let flexibleValue: Any = "or 25";       // safe only after narrowing
Let optionalValue: Int? = null;

Function TypedAdd(Int a, Int b) -> Int {
    Return a + b;
}

Async Function TypedFetch() -> Task<String> {
    Return Await Network.SendText("status");
}

// The compiler and the interpreter both reject a module that violates these rules.

// End of Robix syntax reference.

# Robix Built-in Package Catalogue
## Proposed Standard Library and SDK Package Names

This catalogue is a proposed package namespace specification for Robix. It consolidates the previously proposed built-in packages, corrects acronym capitalization, and adds a dedicated algorithms catalogue.

**Naming convention**
- Package segments use PascalCase for ordinary words: `Robix.Collections`, `Robix.TimeZones`.
- Well-established acronyms retain their conventional uppercase spelling: `HTTP`, `HTTPS`, `TLS`, `SSH`, `TCP`, `UDP`, `DNS`, `JSON`, `XML`, `SQL`, `GPIO`, `I2C`, `SPI`, `UART`, `PWM`, `USB`, `PCI`, `AI`, `ONNX`, `PDF`, `QR`, `HTML`, `CSS`, `DOM`, `ORM`, `FFI`, `API`, `CLI`.
- Conventional mixed-case names remain conventional where appropriate: `WebSocket`, `gRPC`, `WiFi`, `NoSQL`.
- Package availability does not imply that every platform has the required hardware, native library, codec, accelerator, or system service.

GetExceptionStackTrace(exception) -> String
IsCancellation(exception) -> Boolean
IsRecoverable(exception) -> Boolean
HandleException(exception, handler)

Important: Robix should also support structured exception handling:
Try
{
    // Operations
}
Catch<IOException>(error)
{
    // Handle I/O error
}
Catch<Exception>(error)
{
    // Handle other errors
}
Finally
{
    // Cleanup
}

Required language behavior
Robix should support:
Async Function GetNumber() -> Task<Int>
{
    Await Task.Delay(1000);
    Return 100;
}

Async Function Main() -> Task<Void>
{
    Int result = Await GetNumber();
    Print(result);
}

The compiler should generate the necessary asynchronous state machine automatically.

25. Cross-package functionality required by Robix
The 24 packages above also need shared functionality. It should not be duplicated independently in each package.

I recommend adding the following cross-package services.
Component	Responsibility
CoreContracts	Shared interfaces
CoreErrors	Standardized error types
CorePrimitives	Common primitive abstractions
CoreConfiguration	Common configuration
CoreValidation	Shared validation
CoreSerialization	Common serialization interfaces
CoreVersioning	API and runtime versioning
CoreCompatibility	Compatibility checking
CoreCapabilities	Feature and platform detection
CoreResourceManagement	Resource lifecycle
CoreSecurity	Shared security contracts
CoreLogging	Common logging contracts
CoreAsync	Shared asynchronous contracts


26. Recommended standard interfaces
These interfaces should be consistently available across the ecosystem.
Interface IDisposable
{
    Function Dispose() -> Void;
}

Interface IAsyncDisposable
{
    Async Function DisposeAsync() -> Task<Void>;
}

Interface IComparable<T>
{
    Function CompareTo(other: T) -> Int32;
}

Interface IEquatable<T>
{
    Function Equals(other: T) -> Boolean;
}

Interface IEnumerable<T>
{
    Function GetEnumerator() -> IEnumerator<T>;
}

Interface IEnumerator<T>
{
    Property Current: T;

    Function MoveNext() -> Boolean;
    Function Reset() -> Void;
}

Interface IAsyncEnumerable<T>
{
    Function GetAsyncEnumerator(
        cancellationToken: CancellationToken
    ) -> IAsyncEnumerator<T>;
}

Interface IAsyncEnumerator<T>
{
    Property Current: T;

    Async Function MoveNextAsync() -> Task<Boolean>;
    Async Function DisposeAsync() -> Task<Void>;
}

Interface ICloneable<T>
{
    Function Clone() -> T;
}

Interface IValidator<T>
{
    Function Validate(value: T) -> ValidationResult;
}

Interface IFactory<T>
{
    Function Create() -> T;
}

27. Recommended universal API conventions
To make the 24 packages work together, Robix should enforce the following conventions.
Convention	Rule
Naming	PascalCase for classes, interfaces, methods, and properties
Acronyms	Preserve conventional capitalization: HTTP, TLS, SSH, JSON
Generic types	Use <T>, <TKey, TValue>, etc.
Async functions	Use Async and return Task<T> or Task
Cancellation	Accept CancellationToken where cancellation is useful
Errors	Use typed errors or Result<T,E> where appropriate
Resource management	Implement IDisposable or IAsyncDisposable
Null safety	Enforce nullability rules
Type safety	All official APIs must compile under strict typing
Collections	Use standard collection interfaces
Visibility	Support Public, Private, Protected, and Internal
Documentation	Every public class and method must be documented


Important async naming convention
I recommend that Robix support both:
Async Function ReadFile() -> Task<String>
{
    Return Await File.ReadTextAsync("data.txt");
}

And consistent explicit async method names:
ReadTextAsync()
WriteTextAsync()
ConnectAsync()
SendAsync()
ReceiveAsync()
DisposeAsync()

## 2. Mathematics and numerical computing

1. Robix.Math
Responsibility: General mathematical operations shared across the mathematics library.

Cross-package requirements and design rules
The packages above should work together through shared interfaces and consistent conventions. The following are important additions to make the mathematics library coherent rather than a collection of unrelated functions.
A. Shared mathematical interfaces
These interfaces should be available through Robix.Numerics or Robix.Math.
Interface	Responsibility
INumber<T>	Common numeric operations
IInteger<T>	Integer-specific operations
IFloatingPoint<T>	Floating-point operations
IDecimal<T>	Decimal-specific operations
IComplexNumber<T>	Complex-number operations
IVector<T>	Vector operations
IMatrix<T>	Matrix operations
ITensor<T>	Tensor operations
IFunction<TInput,TOutput>	Mathematical function abstraction
IDistribution<T>	Probability distribution abstraction
IRandomGenerator	Random-number generator abstraction
IUnit	Measurement unit abstraction
IQuantity<T>	Physical quantity abstraction
IOptimizer	Optimization algorithm abstraction
IFilter<T>	Signal filter abstraction
ISignal<T>	Signal abstraction
IConvergentMethod	Numerical convergence abstraction
INumericalMethod<T>	Numerical method abstraction


B. Shared enumerations
Recommended common enumerations:
- RoundingMode
- OverflowMode
- UnderflowMode
- NumericCategory
- NumericPrecision
- AngleUnit
- NormType
- MatrixStorageType
- InterpolationMethod
- IntegrationMethod
- DifferentiationMethod
- RootFindingMethod
- OptimizationMethod
- DistributionType
- SamplingMethod
- SignalType
- SignalWindowType
- FilterType
- UnitCategory
- MeasurementSystem
- ConvergenceStatus
- NumericalMethodStatus
C. Shared error types
Mathematical packages should use consistent, typed errors.

Recommended errors:
- MathException
- NumericException
- ArithmeticException
- DivisionByZeroException
- OverflowException
- UnderflowException
- PrecisionLossException
- InvalidNumericValueException
- InvalidArgumentException
- DomainException
- ConvergenceException
- SingularMatrixException
- DimensionMismatchException
- InvalidUnitException
- IncompatibleUnitException
- OptimizationException
- ProbabilityException
- SignalProcessingException
Where practical, functions should also offer non-throwing alternatives that return Result<T, E>.

For example:
Function TrySqrt(value: Float64) -> Result<Float64, MathError>
    If value < 0
        Return Err(MathError.DomainError)
    End

    Return Ok(Math.Sqrt(value))
End

D. Important numerical standards
The implementation should follow these rules:
1. Integer overflow: Provide checked, unchecked, and saturating arithmetic where applicable.
2. Floating-point behavior: Support IEEE 754 special values, including NaN, positive and negative infinity, signed zero, and subnormal numbers.
3. Precision: Do not silently convert arbitrary-precision values into lower-precision floating-point values.
4. Rounding: Provide explicit rounding modes and avoid relying on undocumented defaults.
5. Numerical stability: Prefer stable algorithms for summation, matrix operations, and numerical integration.
6. Randomness: Separate ordinary pseudorandom generation from cryptographically secure random generation.
7. Units: Prevent invalid operations between incompatible physical dimensions.
8. Linear algebra: Support dense and sparse representations.
9. Optimization: Make convergence criteria, iteration limits, and tolerances configurable.
10. Signal processing: Make sample rate, channel count, precision, and filtering behavior explicit.
E. Recommended package dependencies
The package relationships should be organized approximately as follows:
Package	Main dependencies
Robix.Math	Robix.Numerics
Robix.Math.Algebra	Robix.Math, Robix.BigNumbers
Robix.Math.Arithmetic	Robix.Numerics, Robix.BigNumbers
Robix.Math.Geometry	Robix.Math, Robix.Math.LinearAlgebra, Robix.Units
Robix.Math.Trigonometry	Robix.Math, Robix.Numerics
Robix.Math.Statistics	Robix.Math, Robix.Math.Probability
Robix.Math.Probability	Robix.Math.Random, Robix.Math.Statistics
Robix.Math.LinearAlgebra	Robix.Numerics, Robix.Memory
Robix.Math.Calculus	Robix.Math, Robix.Math.Numerical, Robix.Math.LinearAlgebra
Robix.Math.Numerical	Robix.Math, Robix.Numerics, Robix.Math.LinearAlgebra
Robix.Math.Random	Robix.Numerics, Robix.Security.Random for secure random functionality
Robix.Numerics	Robix.Core
Robix.Decimal	Robix.Numerics
Robix.BigNumbers	Robix.Numerics, Robix.Memory
Robix.Units	Robix.Numerics, Robix.Decimal
Robix.Conversions	Robix.Core, Robix.Numerics
Robix.Math.Optimization	Robix.Math, Robix.Math.LinearAlgebra, Robix.Math.Calculus
Robix.Math.SignalProcessing	Robix.Math, Robix.Math.LinearAlgebra, Robix.Math.Random


These are logical dependencies, not mandatory implementation dependencies. For example, Robix.Math.Statistics should be able to use probability distributions without requiring every application using basic statistics to load every probability component.
F. Recommended API conventions
Use these conventions consistently across all 18 packages:
Rule	Convention
Classes	PascalCase
Interfaces	I prefix, e.g. INumber<T>
Methods	PascalCase
Properties	PascalCase
Constants	PascalCase or documented uppercase constants
Generic types	<T>, <TKey, TValue>
Async methods	Async suffix
Non-throwing operations	Try prefix or Result<T,E>
Numeric types	Explicit bit widths
Units	Explicit dimensional types
Precision	Explicit context or options
Errors	Typed error classes
Cancellation	CancellationToken where appropriate

## 3. Algorithms and data structures
# **ROBIX — Algorithms and Data Structures API Specification**

29 packages · Classes, interfaces, structures, algorithms, functions, complexity requirements and implementation rules

This is the proposed **Robix Algorithms Standard Library (RASL)**, covering all 29 packages you provided.

For each package, I will define:

1. **Classes and types:** Classes, structs, enums, interfaces, generic types, and supporting objects.
2. **Functions and methods:** The operations, algorithms, and utilities developers should be able to call.
3. **Advanced functionality:** Additional algorithms required for professional software development.
4. **Implementation contracts:** Type safety, memory usage, mutability, complexity, stability, error handling, and deterministic behavior.

The objective is a comprehensive API specification covering traditional algorithms, modern algorithms, scientific computing, AI, robotics, systems programming, and high-performance computing.

This is a proposed design, not a list of already implemented Robix APIs. It can become the foundation for defining actual compiler-checked function signatures.

## **1.&#x20;**`Robix.Algorithms`

**Responsibility:** Common algorithm interfaces, execution policies, configuration, instrumentation, and reusable algorithm utilities.

### **Classes and types**

| ----------------------------- | ---------------------------------- |

| `AlgorithmChain`              | Algorithm composition              |

| `AlgorithmSelector`           | Automatic algorithm selection      |

| `SpaceComplexity`             | Space-complexity descriptor        |

| `MutationPolicy`              | In-place versus immutable behavior |

| `IAlgorithm<TInput, TOutput>` | Standard algorithm interface       |

## **14.&#x20;**`Robix.Algorithms.Cryptography`

**Responsibility:** Cryptographic algorithm interfaces and integrations with reviewed cryptographic implementations.

**Architecture:** This package should expose vetted algorithms and generic cryptographic interfaces. Higher-level key management, certificate validation, authentication, and TLS handling belong under `Robix.Security`.

### **Classes and types**

- `CryptographicAlgorithm`
- `CryptographicProvider`
- `CryptographicContext`
- `CryptographicPolicy`
- `CryptographicCapabilities`
- `CryptographicResult<T>`
- `CryptographicError`
- `Cipher`
- `SymmetricCipher`
- `AuthenticatedCipher`
- `CipherKey`
- `CipherNonce`
- `CipherTag`
- `CipherParameters`
- `HashAlgorithm`
- `MessageAuthenticationCode`
- `KeyDerivationFunction`
- `PasswordHashFunction`
- `DigitalSignature`
- `SignatureVerifier`
- `PublicKey`
- `PrivateKey`
- `KeyPair`
- `KeyAgreement`
- `KeyEncapsulationMechanism`
- `SecureRandomGenerator`
- `SecureBuffer`
- `CryptographicAlgorithmRegistry`

### **Algorithm implementations**

| Class / Interface  | Purpose                                      |
| ------------------ | -------------------------------------------- |
| `AES`              | Advanced Encryption Standard                 |
| `AESGCM`           | Authenticated AES encryption                 |
| `AESCCM`           | AES Counter with CBC-MAC                     |
| `ChaCha20Poly1305` | Authenticated stream encryption              |
| `SHA256`           | SHA-256 hash                                 |
| `SHA384`           | SHA-384 hash                                 |
| `SHA512`           | SHA-512 hash                                 |
| `SHA3`             | SHA-3 family                                 |
| `SHAKE`            | Extendable-output hash                       |
| `HMAC`             | Keyed message authentication                 |
| `KMAC`             | Keccak message authentication                |
| `HKDF`             | Key derivation                               |
| `PBKDF2`           | Password-based key derivation                |
| `Argon2id`         | Password hashing                             |
| `Scrypt`           | Memory-hard password derivation              |
| `Bcrypt`           | Password hashing                             |
| `RSAOAEP`          | RSA-based encryption                         |
| `RSAPSS`           | RSA signatures                               |
| `ECDSA`            | Elliptic-curve digital signatures            |
| `Ed25519`          | Edwards-curve signatures                     |
| `X25519`           | Elliptic-curve key agreement                 |
| `MLKEM`            | Post-quantum key encapsulation               |
| `MLDSA`            | Post-quantum signatures                      |
| `SLHDSA`           | Stateless hash-based post-quantum signatures |

### **Functions**

**Encryption**

```
Encrypt(plaintext, key, options)
Decrypt(ciphertext, key, options)
EncryptAuthenticated(plaintext, key, nonce, associatedData)
DecryptAuthenticated(ciphertext, key, nonce, tag, associatedData)
GenerateNonce(algorithm)
ValidateCiphertext(ciphertext, parameters)
```

**Hashing**

```
Hash(data, algorithm)
HashStream(stream, algorithm)
HashFile(path, algorithm)
SHA256(data)
SHA384(data)
SHA512(data)
SHA3(data)
ConstantTimeEquals(a, b)
```

**Authentication**

```
ComputeHMAC(key, data, algorithm)
VerifyHMAC(key, data, tag, algorithm)
ComputeKMAC(key, data, options)
VerifyAuthenticationTag(expected, actual)
```

**Key operations**

```
GenerateKey(algorithm)
GenerateKeyPair(algorithm)
DeriveKey(material, salt, options)
HKDFExtract(salt, inputKeyMaterial)
HKDFExpand(prk, info, length)

```

**Signatures**

```
Sign(data, privateKey, algorithm)

```

**Passwords**

```

VerifyPassword(password, encodedHash)
NeedsRehash(encodedHash, policy)
GeneratePasswordSalt()
```

**Security requirements:** Cryptographic functions must use secure randomness, explicit key ownership, authenticated encryption by default, correct nonce handling, and constant-time operations where required. Legacy cryptography should not be silently selected.

### **Ownership rule**

`Robix.Collections` should own the canonical implementations of general-purpose collections such as `List<T>`, `HashMap<K,V>`, and `Queue<T>`.

`Robix.Algorithms.DataStructures` should expose specialized structures and their algorithms without creating duplicate, incompatible implementations of the same collection.

# **30. Shared interfaces for all 29 packages**

The algorithms ecosystem should use a common set of interfaces rather than defining incompatible abstractions inside every package.

| Interface                             | Responsibility                    |
| ------------------------------------- | --------------------------------- |
| `IAlgorithm<TInput,TOutput>`          | General algorithm execution       |
| `IAsyncAlgorithm<TInput,TOutput>`     | Asynchronous algorithm execution  |
| `IParallelAlgorithm<TInput,TOutput>`  | Parallel algorithm execution      |
| `IStreamingAlgorithm<TInput,TOutput>` | Streaming computation             |
| `ICancellableAlgorithm`               | Cancellation support              |
| `IProgressReportingAlgorithm`         | Progress notifications            |
| `ISorter<T>`                          | Sorting contract                  |
| `ISearcher<T>`                        | Searching contract                |
| `ISelector<T>`                        | Selection contract                |
| `IComparer<T>`                        | Value comparison                  |
| `IEqualityComparer<T>`                | Equality and hashing              |
| `IHasher<T>`                          | Hashing operations                |
| `IEncoder<TInput,TOutput>`            | Encoding contract                 |
| `IDecoder<TInput,TOutput>`            | Decoding contract                 |
| `ICompressor`                         | Compression contract              |
| `IDecompressor`                       | Decompression contract            |
| `IGraph<TVertex,TEdge>`               | Graph abstraction                 |
| `ITree<T>`                            | Tree abstraction                  |
| `IHeuristic<T>`                       | Heuristic evaluation              |
| `IOptimizationProblem<T>`             | Optimization problem definition   |
| `IOptimizer<T>`                       | Optimization contract             |
| `IClusteringAlgorithm<T>`             | Clustering contract               |
| `IClassifier<TInput,TLabel>`          | Classification contract           |
| `IRegressor<TInput,TOutput>`          | Regression contract               |
| `IProbabilityDistribution<T>`         | Probability distribution contract |
| `IRandomGenerator`                    | Reproducible randomness           |
| `IDistanceMetric<T>`                  | Distance calculation              |
| `ISimilarityMetric<T>`                | Similarity calculation            |
| `IDataStructure<T>`                   | Common data-structure operations  |

## **Example of a type-safe algorithm interface**

```
Package Robix.Algorithms;


Public Interface IAlgorithm<TInput, TOutput> {

    Public Function Execute(
        TInput input,
        AlgorithmOptions options
    ) -> Result<TOutput, AlgorithmError>;

    Public Async Function ExecuteAsync(
        TInput input,
        AlgorithmOptions options,
        CancellationToken cancellation
    ) -> Task<Result<TOutput, AlgorithmError>>;

    Public Function GetMetadata() -> AlgorithmMetadata;
}
```

An implementation could look like:

```
Package Robix.Algorithms.Sorting;


Import Robix.Algorithms.*;
Import Robix.Collections.*;

Public Class MergeSorter<T>
    Implements IAlgorithm<Array<T>, Array<T>>
    Where T: IComparable<T> {

    Public Function Execute(
        Array<T> input,
        AlgorithmOptions options
    ) -> Result<Array<T>, AlgorithmError> {

        Array<T> result = input.Clone();
        MergeSort(result);

        Return Success(result);
    }

    Public Async Function ExecuteAsync(
        Array<T> input,
        AlgorithmOptions options,
        CancellationToken cancellation
    ) -> Task<Result<Array<T>, AlgorithmError>> {

        cancellation.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();

        Return Await Task.Run(
            Function() => Execute(input, options),
            cancellation
        );
    }

    Public Function GetMetadata() -> AlgorithmMetadata {
        Return AlgorithmMetadata {
            Name: "Merge Sort",
            TimeComplexity: "O(n log n)",
            SpaceComplexity: "O(n)",
            Stable: True,
            InPlace: False,
            Deterministic: True
        };
    }
}
```

This is illustrative Robix syntax. The exact interface, `Explain` documentation format, and task APIs should be aligned with the final compiler grammar.

# **31. Algorithm metadata requirements**

Every public algorithm implementation should provide metadata covering:

| Property                | Meaning                                                                           |
| ----------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Name`                  | Algorithm name                                                                    |
| `Category`              | Package or algorithm family                                                       |
| `Version`               | Implementation version                                                            |
| `TimeComplexityBest`    | Best-case time complexity                                                         |
| `TimeComplexityAverage` | Average-case time complexity                                                      |
| `TimeComplexityWorst`   | Worst-case time complexity                                                        |
| `SpaceComplexity`       | Auxiliary-space requirements                                                      |
| `Stable`                | Whether ordering of equivalent elements is preserved                              |
| `InPlace`               | Whether the implementation mutates its input                                      |
| `Deterministic`         | Whether equivalent inputs produce equivalent results under the stated environment |
| `Parallelizable`        | Whether parallel execution is supported                                           |
| `ThreadSafe`            | Whether simultaneous use is safe                                                  |
| `StreamingSupported`    | Whether streaming input is supported                                              |
| `Cancelable`            | Whether cancellation is supported                                                 |
| `InputRequirements`     | Preconditions and supported data types                                            |
| `OutputGuarantees`      | Guarantees of successful execution                                                |
| `ErrorTypes`            | Possible errors                                                                   |
| `PlatformRequirements`  | Required CPU, GPU, or other capabilities                                          |
| `PrecisionRequirements` | Numerical precision assumptions                                                   |

## **32. Recommended general-purpose algorithm functions**

For the convenience of developers, the library should expose a small, canonical high-level API while keeping specialized algorithms in their respective packages.

```
Import Robix.Algorithms.*;
Import Robix.Algorithms.Sorting As Sorting;
Import Robix.Algorithms.Searching As Searching;
Import Robix.Algorithms.Graphs As Graphs;
Import Robix.Algorithms.DataStructures As Structures;
```

Developers could then write:

```

Array<Int> numbers = [9, 4, 7, 1, 5, 2];

// Sort the array.
Sorting.Sort(numbers);

// Search a sorted array.
Option<Int> index = Searching.BinarySearch(numbers, 5);

// Find the three largest values.
Array<Int> largest = Selection.TopK(numbers, 3);

// Create and use a graph.
Graph<Int, Float> graph = Graph<Int, Float>();

graph.AddVertex(1);
graph.AddVertex(2);
graph.AddEdge(1, 2, 5.0);

GraphPath<Int> path = Graphs.ShortestPath(graph, 1, 2);

// Create a heap.
MinHeap<Int> heap = MinHeap<Int>();

heap.Insert(10);
heap.Insert(3);
heap.Insert(8);

Int smallest = heap.ExtractMin();
```

# **33. Required rules for the Robix algorithms library**

I recommend making these rules compulsory for official algorithm implementations.

1. **Strict type safety:** Every package must compile under strict typing.
2. **Generic support:** Algorithms should work with arbitrary compatible numeric and comparable types.
3. **Explicit mutation:** APIs should distinguish in-place operations from operations returning new collections.
4. **Deterministic behavior:** Algorithms should document their behavior and accept explicit seeds when using randomness.
5. **Complexity documentation:** Public algorithms must document time and space complexity, including important preconditions.
6. **Error handling:** Recoverable failures should use typed errors or `Result<T,E>`.
7. **Async support:** Long-running or I/O-bound algorithms should support asynchronous execution where useful.
8. **Cancellation:** Expensive computations should support `CancellationToken`.
9. **Thread safety:** Parallel algorithms must document shared-state and callback requirements.
10. **Memory safety:** Algorithms must respect collection ownership, bounds checking, and safe resource management.
11. **No redundant implementations:** Shared foundational algorithms should have one canonical implementation.
12. **Stable interfaces:** Implementations may improve without unnecessarily breaking public API compatibility.
13. **Secure implementations:** Cryptographic functions must rely on maintained and reviewed implementations.
14. **Input validation:** Algorithms must reject invalid parameters and unsupported input configurations clearly.
15. **Platform detection:** GPU and hardware-accelerated algorithms must check capabilities before execution.


## 4. Text, strings, and data formats

# **ROBIX — Text, Strings, and Data Formats API Specification**

21 packages · Classes · Structures · Interfaces · Functions · Encoders · Parsers · Serializers · Security · Async APIs

This is the proposed **Robix Text and Data Standard Library (RTDSL)**, following the same approach as the previous Core, Mathematics, and Algorithms specifications.

It covers all 21 packages you provided, including their classes, types, interfaces, functions, advanced features, and recommended implementation rules.

The API design should satisfy these requirements:

- **Mandatory type safety:** Every official implementation must support strict typing.
- **Synchronous and asynchronous APIs:** Especially for files, streams, large documents, compression, and archives.
- **Full Unicode support:** Including UTF-8, UTF-16, UTF-32, grapheme clusters, normalization, and international text.
- **Streaming:** Large files should not need to be loaded entirely into memory.
- **Error handling:** Typed errors through `Result<T, E>`, with exceptions where appropriate.
- **Security:** Safe parsing, bounded resource consumption, and protection against malicious input.
- **Interoperability:** Support recognized format and protocol standards.

These are proposed Robix interfaces, not claims about an already implemented library. This specification aims for comprehensive coverage, but final API completeness will depend on the supported standards, platforms, and Robix runtime.

## **1.&#x20;**`Robix.String`

**Responsibility:** Fundamental string creation, inspection, manipulation, comparison, searching, conversion, and memory-efficient processing.

**Security:** Extraction must prevent path traversal, absolute-path escape, unsafe symbolic links, decompression bombs, and uncontrolled file overwrites. Not every archive format supports writing or encryption; availability must be reported accurately.

**Integer operations**

```

**Endianness**

```

**Packing**

```

```

**Variable-length encoding**

```

```

**Checksums**

```
Binary.CRC32(data)
Binary.CRC64(data)
Binary.Adler32(data)
Binary.Fletcher32(data)
Binary.VerifyChecksum(data, expected, algorithm)
```

**Strict type safety:** Every read and write should validate bounds. Unsafe memory reinterpretation should require an explicit `Unsafe` block, and byte order must be explicit for portable binary formats.

**Memory safety:** Pooled and non-owning buffers require explicit lifetime rules. A returned pool buffer must not remain accessible through outstanding mutable views after its lease ends.

# **22. Shared interfaces for all 21 packages**

To ensure every package works with the others, Robix should define common interfaces rather than introducing incompatible readers, writers, serializers, and encoders.

| Interface                  | Responsibility           |
| -------------------------- | ------------------------ |
| `ITextReader`              | Sequential text reading  |
| `ITextWriter`              | Sequential text writing  |
| `IStringConvertible<T>`    | String conversion        |
| `IStringFormattable`       | Custom string formatting |
| `ITextParser<T>`           | Typed text parsing       |
| `ITextFormatter<T>`        | Typed text formatting    |
| `IEncoder<TInput,TOutput>` | Encoding                 |
| `IDecoder<TInput,TOutput>` | Decoding                 |
| `IIncrementalEncoder`      | Incremental encoding     |
| `IIncrementalDecoder`      | Incremental decoding     |
| `IParser<T>`               | General parsing          |
| `IStreamingParser<T>`      | Streaming parsing        |
| `ISerializer<T>`           | Object serialization     |
| `IDeserializer<T>`         | Object deserialization   |
| `ISchemaValidator<T>`      | Schema validation        |
| `ICompressor`              | Compression              |
| `IDecompressor`            | Decompression            |
| `IArchiveReader`           | Archive reading          |
| `IArchiveWriter`           | Archive writing          |
| `IBinaryReader`            | Binary reading           |
| `IBinaryWriter`            | Binary writing           |
| `IBuffer<T>`               | Buffer access            |
| `IBufferWriter<T>`         | Buffer output            |
| `ICodec<TInput,TOutput>`   | General codec            |
| `IMIMEProvider`            | Media-type lookup        |
| `IAsyncDisposable`         | Async resource disposal  |

## **23. Required API conventions**

| Concern            | Required convention                                           |
| ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Type safety        | strict typing for compiled code                         |
| Classes            | PascalCase                                                    |
| Acronyms           | `JSON`, `XML`, `YAML`, `TOML`, `CSV`, `MIME`, `UTF8`, `ASCII` |
| Async APIs         | `Async` suffix and `Task<T>` return types                     |
| Fallible parsing   | `TryParse` or `Result<T, E>`                                  |
| Immutable strings  | `String` should be immutable                                  |
| Mutable text       | Use `StringBuilder` or `MutableString`                        |
| Large files        | Support stream-based processing                               |
| Character encoding | UTF-8 default, explicit alternatives                          |
| Memory management  | Explicit buffer ownership and disposal                        |
| Resource limits    | Configurable size, depth, and time limits                     |
| Serialization      | Explicit type contracts                                       |
| Format compliance  | Document supported standard versions                          |
| Security           | Safe-by-default parsing and deserialization                   |

## **24. Recommended error hierarchy**

```
TextError
    StringError
    UnicodeError
    EncodingError
    FormattingError
    ParsingError
    RegexError
    TemplateError

DataFormatError
    JSONError
    XMLError
    YAMLError
    TOMLError
    CSVError
    MIMEError

SerializationError
    SchemaValidationError
    UnsupportedTypeError
    CircularReferenceError
    UnsafeDeserializationError

BinaryError
    BufferBoundsError
    InvalidBinaryLayoutError
    EndiannessError

CompressionError
    UnsupportedCompressionError
    CorruptCompressedDataError
    DecompressionLimitError

ArchiveError
    CorruptArchiveError
    UnsupportedArchiveFormatError
    UnsafeArchivePathError

CodecError
    UnsupportedCodecError
    InvalidEncodedDataError
```

All these error types should have the common fields `Code`, `Message`, `Details`, and optional `InnerError`.

## **25. Example Robix application**

The following illustrates how these libraries could be used together.

```
Package Robotics.DataProcessor;


Import Robix.String As RoboString;
Import Robix.Text As Text;
Import Robix.JSON As JSON;
Import Robix.CSV As CSV;
Import Robix.XML As XML;
Import Robix.Compression As Compression;
Import Robix.Archives As Archives;
Import Robix.Text.Encoding As Encoding;
Import Robix.Text.RegularExpressions As Regex;
Import Robix.IO.Files As Files;

Public Async Function ProcessData()
    -> Task<Result<Void, DataFormatError>>
Explain {
    Description: "Process and export robot data.";
    Input: None;
    Processing: "Read, parse, filter, serialize, and compress data.";
    Output: "Success or a typed data processing error.";
} {
    Try {
        // Read file asynchronously.
        String input = Await Files.ReadTextAsync("robots.json");

        // Parse JSON into a typed collection.
        Array<RobotData> robots =
            JSON.Deserialize<Array<RobotData>>(input);

        // Filter the robots.
        Array<RobotData> active =
            robots.Filter(Function(robot) => robot.Active);

        // Convert records to CSV.
        String csvText = CSV.Serialize(active);

        // Write the results.
        Await Files.WriteTextAsync("active.csv", csvText);

        // Compress the output.
        Array<Byte> compressed =
            Compression.Gzip(
                Encoding.EncodeUTF8(csvText)
            );

        Await Files.WriteBytesAsync(
            "active.csv.gz",
            compressed
        );

        Return Success();
    }
    Catch (DataFormatError error) {
        Return Failure(error);
    }
}
```

## **26. Recommended package ownership and dependencies**

| Package                         | Main dependencies                                      |
| ------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |
| `Robix.String`                  | `Robix.Core`, `Robix.Text.Unicode`                     |
| `Robix.Text`                    | `Robix.String`                                         |
| `Robix.Text.Encoding`           | `Robix.Buffers`, `Robix.Text.Unicode`                  |
| `Robix.Text.Unicode`            | `Robix.Core`                                           |
| `Robix.Text.RegularExpressions` | `Robix.String`, `Robix.Text`                           |
| `Robix.Text.Parsing`            | `Robix.Text`                                           |
| `Robix.Text.Formatting`         | `Robix.String`, `Robix.Globalization`                  |
| `Robix.Text.Markdown`           | `Robix.Text.Parsing`                                   |
| `Robix.Text.Templates`          | `Robix.Text.Parsing`, `Robix.Text.Formatting`          |
| `Robix.JSON`                    | `Robix.Text.Encoding`, `Robix.Serialization` contracts |
| `Robix.XML`                     | `Robix.Text.Encoding`, `Robix.Serialization` contracts |
| `Robix.YAML`                    | `Robix.Text.Encoding`, `Robix.Serialization` contracts |
| `Robix.TOML`                    | `Robix.Text.Encoding`, `Robix.Serialization` contracts |
| `Robix.CSV`                     | `Robix.Text`, `Robix.Text.Encoding`                    |
| `Robix.Serialization`           | `Robix.Core`, serialization contracts                  |
| `Robix.Compression`             | `Robix.Buffers`, algorithm backends                    |
| `Robix.Archives`                | `Robix.IO`, `Robix.Compression`                        |
| `Robix.Binary`                  | `Robix.Buffers`, `Robix.Numerics`                      |
| `Robix.Buffers`                 | `Robix.Memory`, `Robix.Core`                           |
| `Robix.Codecs`                  | Codec interfaces, individual format providers          |
| `Robix.MIME`                    | `Robix.String`, `Robix.Text.Parsing`                   |

These are logical relationships rather than required direct dependencies. In particular, `Robix.Serialization` should define contracts independently enough that format packages can implement those contracts without circular dependencies.

## **27. Important design decisions**

Before implementation, these decisions should be finalized:

1. Whether `String` indexing refers to Unicode code points, code units, bytes, or grapheme clusters.
2. Whether string slices borrow the original string's storage or copy their contents.
3. Which Unicode version Robix supports.
4. Whether format parsers preserve comments and source formatting where those features are permitted.
5. Whether JSON numeric values retain arbitrary precision.
6. Which YAML and TOML specification versions are supported.
7. Whether serializers require compile-time generated metadata or permit reflection.
8. Which regular-expression features are supported by each execution engine.
9. Whether all streaming APIs follow a common backpressure contract.
10. Which archive formats support reading, writing, encryption, and streaming.
11. How pooled buffers prevent use-after-release.
12. Which codecs ship by default and which require optional native providers.

### **Final recommendation**

I recommend treating `Robix.String`**,&#x20;**`Robix.Text.Unicode`**,&#x20;**`Robix.Text.Encoding`**,&#x20;**`Robix.Buffers`**, and&#x20;**`Robix.Binary`**&#x20;as foundational layers**.

Build the parsers and format libraries on top of these foundations, with shared serialization and streaming contracts. That makes it possible to support additional formats such as CBOR, MessagePack, Protocol Buffers, Apache Avro, and Apache Parquet later without redesigning the standard library.

## 5. Filesystem, I/O, and storage

# **ROBIX — Filesystem, I/O, and Storage API Specification**

17 packages · Classes · Structures · Interfaces · Functions · Async APIs · Storage providers · Transactions · Security

This specification covers all **17 Filesystem, I/O, and Storage packages** you listed, following the same approach as the previous Robix API catalogues.

Each package includes its proposed classes, structures, interfaces, functions, and advanced capabilities.

The API design should follow these principles:

- **Mandatory type safety:** Every official package must support strict typing during compilation.
- **Synchronous and asynchronous operations:** File, network-backed storage, database, and stream operations should provide async alternatives.
- **Platform independence:** Portable APIs must work across Windows, Linux, and macOS wherever possible.
- **Resource safety:** Files, streams, database connections, and transactions must support deterministic disposal.
- **Error handling:** Recoverable failures should use typed errors such as `Result<T, IOError>`.
- **Concurrency:** Support cancellation tokens, file locking, atomic operations, and safe concurrent access.
- **Performance:** Support buffering, pooling, memory mapping, zero-copy operations, and streaming large files.
- **Security:** Include permission checking, path validation, secure temporary files, and protection against resource exhaustion.

These APIs are proposed for Robix; they are not claims of existing implementation. The catalogue aims for comprehensive functional coverage, though a literally final API requires agreed runtime, storage, and platform contracts.

```

**Required design:** Backpressure is compulsory for bounded pipelines. Cancellation, errors, and completion must propagate predictably through all stages, and disposable resources must be released when processing terminates.

Paths.CommonPrefix(paths)
```

**Validation**

```
Paths.IsValid(path)
Paths.Validate(path)
Paths.IsRooted(path)
Paths.IsUnderRoot(path, root)
Paths.ContainsTraversal(path)
Paths.IsReservedName(name)
Paths.GetInvalidCharacters(platform)
Paths.SanitizeFileName(name)
```

**Comparison and matching**

```
Paths.Equals(a, b, options)
Paths.Compare(a, b, options)
Paths.MatchGlob(path, pattern)
Paths.GetRelativeDifference(a, b)
Paths.IsAncestor(parent, child)
Paths.IsDescendant(child, parent)
```

**Special directories**

```
Paths.GetCurrentDirectory()
Paths.GetHomeDirectory()
Paths.GetTemporaryDirectory()
Paths.GetApplicationDataDirectory()
Paths.GetCacheDirectory()
Paths.GetConfigurationDirectory()
Paths.GetExecutableDirectory()
```

**File URI handling**

```
Paths.ToFileURI(path)
Paths.FromFileURI(uri)
Paths.IsFileURI(uri)
```

**Security:** Lexical path normalization is not equivalent to secure filesystem resolution. Protection against symlink traversal and time-of-check/time-of-use races requires filesystem-aware operations, directory handles, and suitable platform facilities.

## **11.&#x20;**`Robix.Storage.Database`
