# **18. Shared interfaces for all 17 packages**

Every package should reuse common contracts wherever possible.

| Interface                        | Responsibility                  |
| -------------------------------- | ------------------------------- |
| `IReadable`                      | Read operations                 |
| `IWritable`                      | Write operations                |
| `ISeekable`                      | Position-based operations       |
| `IStream`                        | General stream abstraction      |
| `IAsyncStream`                   | Asynchronous stream abstraction |
| `IFileSystem`                    | Filesystem operations           |
| `IFile`                          | File abstraction                |
| `IDirectory`                     | Directory abstraction           |
| `IFileWatcher`                   | File change notifications       |
| `IBuffer<T>`                     | Buffer operations               |
| `IBufferPool<T>`                 | Pooled memory                   |
| `IPipelineStage<TInput,TOutput>` | Pipeline processing             |
| `IStorage`                       | General storage abstraction     |
| `IStorageProvider`               | Storage provider                |
| `ITransactionalStorage`          | Transactional storage           |
| `IVersionedStorage`              | Version-aware storage           |
| `IStorageSerializer<T>`          | Typed storage serialization     |
| `ICache<TKey,TValue>`            | Cache operations                |
| `IKeyValueStore<TKey,TValue>`    | Key-value storage               |
| `IConfiguration`                 | Configuration operations        |
| `IConfigurationProvider`         | Configuration source            |
| `IDatabaseProvider`              | Database provider               |
| `IDatabaseConnection`            | Database connection             |
| `IDatabaseTransaction`           | Database transaction            |
| `IDatabaseQuery<T>`              | Typed database querying         |
| `IDisposable`                    | Deterministic resource disposal |
| `IAsyncDisposable`               | Asynchronous disposal           |

## **19. Required error hierarchy**

I recommend the following shared error categories:

```
IOError
    FileNotFoundError
    DirectoryNotFoundError
    PermissionDeniedError
    FileAlreadyExistsError
    PathTooLongError
    InvalidPathError
    EndOfStreamError
    StreamClosedError
    DeviceUnavailableError
    IOErrorTimeout
    IOCancelledError

StorageError
    StorageUnavailableError
    StorageNotFoundError
    StorageConflictError
    StorageQuotaExceededError
    StorageReadOnlyError
    StorageCorruptionError
    StorageTransactionError
    StorageVersionMismatchError

CacheError
    CacheUnavailableError
    CacheSerializationError
    CacheCapacityError

ConfigurationError
    ConfigurationNotFoundError
    ConfigurationValidationError
    ConfigurationBindingError
    ConfigurationProviderError

DatabaseError
    DatabaseConnectionError
    DatabaseQueryError
    DatabaseTimeoutError
    DatabaseConstraintError
    DatabaseTransactionError
    DatabaseAuthenticationError
    DatabaseAuthorizationError
    DatabaseSchemaError
    DatabaseMigrationError
    DatabaseProviderError
```

Every error should expose a stable error code, message, optional underlying error, and contextual metadata. Sensitive information such as passwords or database credentials must never appear in diagnostic messages.

# **20. Common API conventions**

| Requirement         | Convention                                                               |
| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Type safety         | strict typing mandatory for compiled modules                       |
| Naming              | PascalCase for public types and functions                                |
| Acronyms            | Uppercase `IO`, `SQL`, `JSON`, `XML`, `YAML`, `TOML`, `TCP`, `TLS`, etc. |
| Async methods       | `Async` suffix and `Task<T>` return type                                 |
| Fallible operations | `Try` variants or `Result<T,E>`                                          |
| Cancellation        | `CancellationToken`                                                      |
| Resource cleanup    | `IDisposable` or `IAsyncDisposable`                                      |
| Large data          | Streaming and bounded buffering                                          |
| File paths          | `Robix.Paths` abstractions                                               |
| Queries             | Parameterized or typed query representations                             |
| Transactions        | Explicit commit and rollback                                             |
| Persistence         | Clearly documented durability guarantees                                 |
| Concurrency         | Document isolation and thread safety                                     |
| Security            | Least-privilege access and validated inputs                              |

## **21. Example: Type-safe asynchronous file processing**

```
Package Robotics.DataStorage;


Import Robix.IO.Files As Files;
Import Robix.IO.Streams As Streams;
Import Robix.Paths As Paths;
Import Robix.Storage.Local As Local;
Import Robix.Storage.Cache As Cache;
Import Robix.JSON As JSON;

Public Async Function SaveRobotData(
    String robotId,
    RobotData data,
    CancellationToken cancellation
) -> Task<Result<Void, StorageError>>
Explain {
    Description: "Store robot data in local storage.";
    Input:
        robotId = "Unique robot identifier",
        data = "Robot data to save",
        cancellation = "Cancellation token";
    Processing: "Serialize and save the data asynchronously.";
    Output: "Success or a typed storage error.";
} {
    Try {
        String filename = robotId + ".json";
        String path = Paths.Join("robots", filename);

        String json = JSON.Serialize(data);

        Await Files.WriteTextAsync(
            path,
            json,
            cancellation
        );

        Return Success();
    }
    Catch (StorageError error) {
        Return Failure(error);
    }
}
```

For a fully type-safe implementation, file-related errors such as `IOError` should be mapped explicitly into the declared `StorageError` return type, rather than relying on implicit conversion.

## **22. Recommended package ownership**

Avoid creating multiple incompatible implementations of the same functionality.

| Canonical owner               | What it owns                            |
| ----------------------------- | --------------------------------------- |
| `Robix.IO`                    | General reader/writer and I/O contracts |
| `Robix.IO.Files`              | File operations                         |
| `Robix.IO.Directories`        | Directory operations                    |
| `Robix.IO.Streams`            | Stream implementations                  |
| `Robix.IO.Buffers`            | I/O-specific buffer integration         |
| `Robix.IO.Pipelines`          | Pipeline processing                     |
| `Robix.Paths`                 | Path operations                         |
| `Robix.Storage`               | Generic storage contracts               |
| `Robix.Storage.Memory`        | In-memory storage providers             |
| `Robix.Storage.Local`         | Local persistent storage                |
| `Robix.Storage.Database`      | Storage API backed by databases         |
| `Robix.Storage.Cache`         | Caching                                 |
| `Robix.Storage.KeyValue`      | Key-value storage                       |
| `Robix.Storage.Configuration` | Configuration providers                 |
| `Robix.Databases`             | Database connection and query contracts |
| `Robix.Databases.SQL`         | Relational database APIs                |
| `Robix.Databases.NoSQL`       | Non-relational database APIs            |

### **Final architecture recommendation**

Robix should treat `Robix.IO`**,&#x20;**`Robix.IO.Streams`**,&#x20;**`Robix.Paths`**, and&#x20;**`Robix.Storage`**&#x20;as the foundational abstractions** for this category.

Filesystem packages should build on the I/O and path foundations. Storage providers should implement `Robix.Storage` contracts. Database-backed storage should reuse database providers, avoiding duplicate implementations.


## 6. Networking and communication

# **ROBIX — Networking and Communication API Specification**

21 packages · Classes · Structures · Interfaces · Functions · Async APIs · Protocols · Security · Networking infrastructure

This is the proposed **Robix Networking and Communication Standard Library (RNCSL)**, covering all 21 packages you listed.

The specification is designed for client and server applications, cloud systems, distributed computing, network automation, robotics, industrial devices, and embedded applications.

Every package should follow these common requirements:

- **Strict type safety:** Official APIs must support strict typing.
- **Asynchronous I/O:** Network operations should support `Async`, `Await`, `Task<T>`, and `CancellationToken`.
- **Secure defaults:** TLS certificate validation, protected credentials, and bounded network operations.
- **Cross-platform behavior:** Explicitly report unsupported operating-system or hardware features.
- **Typed errors:** Distinguish transport, protocol, authentication, timeout, cancellation, and validation failures.
- **Resource safety:** Connections, sockets, channels, and streams support deterministic disposal.
- **Extensibility:** Shared interfaces should allow custom transports and protocol implementations.

This is a proposed API design, not a claim that these packages are already implemented. The function inventories are intended as a comprehensive implementation baseline.

## **1.&#x20;**`Robix.Network`

**Responsibility:** Fundamental networking abstractions, addresses, endpoints, interfaces, network state, diagnostics, and common network operations.

### **Classes and types**

**Core networking**

- `Network`
- `NetworkManager`
- `NetworkContext`
- `NetworkEnvironment`
- `NetworkConfiguration`
- `NetworkOptions`
- `NetworkCapabilities`

## **3.&#x20;**`Robix.Network.HTTPS`

**Responsibility:** Secure HTTP endpoint configuration, TLS-enabled HTTP clients and servers, certificate policies, and secure transport integration.

HTTPS should not contain an independent HTTP implementation. It should compose `Robix.Network.HTTP` with `Robix.Network.TLS`.

### **Classes and types**

- `HTTPSClient`
- `HTTPSServer`
- `HTTPSConnection`
- `HTTPSConnectionPool`
- `HTTPSClientOptions`
- `HTTPSServerOptions`
- `HTTPSRequestOptions`
- `HTTPSResponse`
- `HTTPSListener`
- `HTTPSContext`
- `HTTPSCertificate`
- `HTTPSCertificateChain`

- `HTTPSCertificatePolicy`
- `HTTPSClientCertificate`
- `HTTPSClientAuthentication`
- `HTTPSRedirectPolicy`
- `HTTPSSecurityOptions`
- `HTTPSHandshakeInfo`
- `HTTPSConnectionInfo`
- `HTTPSMetrics`
- `HTTPSError`
- `HTTPSCertificateError`
- `HTTPSHandshakeError`

### **Functions**

**Secure clients**

```

HTTPS.CreateClientWithCertificate(certificate)
HTTPS.ConfigureTLS(client, tlsOptions)
HTTPS.SetTrustedCertificates(client, store)
HTTPS.SetClientCertificate(client, certificate)
HTTPS.GetTLSInformation(client)
```

**Requests**

```

```

**Certificate operations**

```

```

**Server operations**

```

HTTPS.StopServerAsync(server)

HTTPS.ConfigureHSTS(server, policy)
```

**Connection inspection**

```

HTTPS.GetPeerCertificate(connection)

```

**Security requirement:** Certificate and hostname validation must be enabled by default. Unsafe testing overrides must not silently become production configuration.

**Important:** TCP is a byte stream, not a message protocol. `Send` and `Receive` must not imply message boundaries. Applications needing messages must use framing or a higher-level protocol.


**Record queries**

```

```

**Cache**


**DNSSEC**


**Zone operations**

```

```

**Diagnostics**

```

DNS.TraceResolution(name)
DNS.GetResolverStatistics()
DNS.GetLastResponseCode()
```

DNS queries should have configurable retry, timeout, recursion, response-size, and cache policies. Secure resolver transport is not the same as DNSSEC validation.

## **9.&#x20;**`Robix.Network.FTP`


**Security:** Plain FTP transmits credentials and data without transport encryption. The standard library should discourage it on untrusted networks and direct users toward SFTP or an authenticated TLS-protected transfer method.


SFTP is not FTP over TLS. It is a separate file-transfer protocol carried over SSH. Server extensions, atomic renames, and remote copy operations must be capability-gated.

**Required security defaults:** Prefer TLS 1.3 with securely configured TLS 1.2 compatibility where needed. Certificate-chain and hostname validation must not be disabled implicitly. TLS libraries should handle nonce generation, key schedules, and handshake authentication internally.

- `MessagingMetrics`
- `MessagingStatistics`

**Protocol providers**

- `MQTTClient`
- `MQTTBroker`
- `AMQPClient`
- `NATSClient`
- `KafkaClient`
- `STOMPClient`
- `MessagingProvider`
- `MessagingProviderRegistry`

### **Functions**

**Connection**

```
Messaging.Connect(provider, options)
Messaging.ConnectAsync(provider, options)
Messaging.Disconnect(client)
Messaging.DisconnectAsync(client)
Messaging.IsConnected(client)
Messaging.ReconnectAsync(client)
```

**Publishing**

```
Messaging.Publish(topic, message)
Messaging.PublishAsync(topic, message)
Messaging.PublishBatch(topic, messages)
Messaging.PublishBatchAsync(topic, messages)

```

```

```

```

```

**Queues and topics**

```

Messaging.DeleteQueue(name)

Messaging.DeleteTopic(name)

```

**Delivery policies**

```

```

**Transactions and checkpoints**

```

Messaging.Rollback(transaction)

Messaging.GetCommittedOffset(consumer)
Messaging.CreateCheckpoint(consumer)
Messaging.RestoreCheckpoint(consumer, checkpoint)
```

**Inspection**

```
Messaging.GetMessageMetadata(message)
Messaging.GetDeliveryCount(message)
Messaging.GetBrokerCapabilities()
Messaging.GetStatistics()
Messaging.GetConsumerLag(consumer)
```

**Important:** At-most-once, at-least-once, and effectively-once processing are different contracts. Exactly-once business effects generally require coordinated application state, not merely a message-broker setting.


**RPC correctness:** Network failure may occur after a remote operation has executed but before its response is received. Retrying calls should require explicit idempotency semantics.

**Safety:** Hardware control APIs must not assume that receiving a frame proves its authenticity. CAN traffic can be safety-critical, so diagnostics, permissions, rate limits, and device-specific safeguards should be built into the API.

**Important:** QUIC uses TLS 1.3 for its security handshake but is not simply TLS layered over TCP. QUIC operates over UDP and has its own transport security integration, streams, congestion control, and connection identifiers. Zero-RTT application data requires explicit replay-risk handling.

# **22. Shared interfaces for all 21 networking packages**

Robix should define common interfaces so that transport protocols, secure connections, client/server libraries, and messaging systems can interoperate.

| Interface                 | Responsibility                    |
| ------------------------- | --------------------------------- |
| `INetworkTransport`       | Generic network transport         |
| `IConnection`             | Connection lifecycle              |
| `IAsyncConnection`        | Asynchronous connection lifecycle |
| `IConnectionFactory`      | Connection creation               |
| `IConnectionPool`         | Connection pooling                |
| `IListener`               | Incoming connection handling      |
| `IEndpoint`               | Endpoint abstraction              |
| `IAddressResolver`        | Address resolution                |
| `IStreamTransport`        | Byte-stream transport             |
| `IDatagramTransport`      | Datagram transport                |
| `ISecureTransport`        | Secure transport                  |
| `ICertificateValidator`   | Certificate validation            |
| `IAuthenticationProvider` | Authentication                    |
| `IProxyConnector`         | Proxy connections                 |
| `IHTTPRequestHandler`     | HTTP request handling             |
| `IHTTPMiddleware`         | HTTP middleware                   |
| `IWebSocketHandler`       | WebSocket messages                |
| `IMessagePublisher<T>`    | Message publishing                |
| `IMessageSubscriber<T>`   | Message subscription              |
| `IMessageSerializer<T>`   | Message serialization             |
| `IRPCClient`              | RPC client operations             |
| `IRPCService`             | RPC service implementations       |
| `IDiscoveryProvider`      | Service discovery                 |
| `IMulticastTransport`     | Multicast transport               |
| `ICANController`          | CAN hardware abstraction          |
| `IQUICConnection`         | QUIC connection operations        |
| `INetworkDiagnostics`     | Network diagnostics               |
| `INetworkStatistics`      | Network statistics                |

## **23. Recommended error hierarchy**

```
NetworkError
    NetworkUnavailableError
    NetworkTimeoutError
    NetworkPermissionError
    NetworkAddressError
    NetworkResolutionError
    NetworkConnectionError
    NetworkDisconnectedError
    NetworkProtocolError
    NetworkAuthenticationError
    NetworkCancellationError

HTTPError
    HTTPRequestError
    HTTPResponseError
    HTTPProtocolError
    HTTPTimeoutError
    HTTPRedirectError

TLSError
    TLSHandshakeError
    TLSCertificateError
    TLSAuthenticationError
    TLSProtocolError

SSHError
    SSHAuthenticationError
    SSHHostKeyError
    SSHConnectionError
    SSHChannelError

DNSError
    DNSResolutionError
    DNSResponseError
    DNSTimeoutError
    DNSSECValidationError

FTPError
    FTPAuthenticationError
    FTPTransferError
    FTPProtocolError

SFTPError
    SFTPAuthenticationError
    SFTPFileError
    SFTPTransferError

WebSocketError
    WebSocketHandshakeError
    WebSocketProtocolError
    WebSocketClosedError

MessagingError
    MessageDeliveryError
    MessageSerializationError
    MessageAcknowledgementError

RPCError
    RPCTransportError
    RPCDeadlineExceededError
    RPCServiceUnavailableError

CANError
    CANBusError
    CANControllerError
    CANFrameError

QUICError
    QUICTransportError
    QUICStreamError
    QUICHandshakeError
    QUICApplicationError
```

Each error should expose `Code`, `Message`, `Details`, optional `InnerError`, and an indication of whether retrying is potentially appropriate.

# **24. Shared networking configuration types**

| Type                           | Purpose                                   |
| ------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------- |
| `NetworkTimeoutOptions`        | Connect, read, write, and total deadlines |
| `NetworkRetryPolicy`           | Retry behavior and backoff                |
| `NetworkCancellationOptions`   | Cancellation behavior                     |
| `NetworkBufferOptions`         | Buffer sizes and ownership                |
| `NetworkRateLimitOptions`      | Bandwidth and request limits              |
| `NetworkSecurityPolicy`        | Security requirements                     |
| `NetworkAuthenticationOptions` | Authentication configuration              |
| `NetworkProxyOptions`          | Proxy configuration                       |
| `NetworkLoggingOptions`        | Logging and redaction                     |
| `NetworkTracingOptions`        | Distributed tracing                       |
| `NetworkMetricsOptions`        | Connection and transport metrics          |
| `NetworkKeepAliveOptions`      | Idle connection handling                  |
| `NetworkConnectionPoolOptions` | Connection reuse                          |
| `NetworkBackpressureOptions`   | Bounded pending-data policies             |

## **25. Common networking API conventions**

| Requirement      | Rule                                                                                             |
| ---------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Type safety      | All official APIs must support strict typing                                               |
| Classes          | PascalCase                                                                                       |
| Acronyms         | Preserve `HTTP`, `HTTPS`, `TCP`, `UDP`, `DNS`, `FTP`, `SFTP`, `SSH`, `TLS`, `RPC`, `CAN`, `QUIC` |
| gRPC             | Preserve the conventional mixed-case spelling                                                    |
| Async APIs       | Use `Async` suffix and `Task<T>`                                                                 |
| Cancellation     | Accept `CancellationToken`                                                                       |
| Timeouts         | Separate connection, operation, and total deadlines                                              |
| Security         | Authenticate endpoints and validate certificates by default                                      |
| Resources        | Implement `IDisposable` and `IAsyncDisposable`                                                   |
| Buffers          | Use `Robix.Buffers` abstractions                                                                 |
| Streams          | Use `Robix.IO.Streams` abstractions                                                              |
| Errors           | Typed error codes and recoverable results                                                        |
| Platform support | Capability-gated native features                                                                 |
| Observability    | Connection metrics, tracing, and redacted logs                                                   |

## **26. Recommended package dependency architecture**

| Package                   | Main dependencies                                                                |
| ------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Robix.Network`           | `Robix.Core`, `Robix.Async`, `Robix.IO`                                          |
| `Robix.Network.HTTP`      | `Robix.Network.TCP`, `Robix.Network.TLS`, `Robix.Network.QUIC` as supported      |
| `Robix.Network.HTTPS`     | `Robix.Network.HTTP`, `Robix.Network.TLS`                                        |
| `Robix.Network.WebSocket` | `Robix.Network.HTTP`, `Robix.Network.Sockets`                                    |
| `Robix.Network.TCP`       | `Robix.Network.Sockets`                                                          |
| `Robix.Network.UDP`       | `Robix.Network.Sockets`                                                          |
| `Robix.Network.Sockets`   | `Robix.Network`, native OS backends                                              |
| `Robix.Network.DNS`       | `Robix.Network.UDP`, `Robix.Network.TCP` and optional secure resolver transports |
| `Robix.Network.FTP`       | `Robix.Network.TCP`                                                              |
| `Robix.Network.SFTP`      | `Robix.Network.SSH`                                                              |
| `Robix.Network.SSH`       | `Robix.Network.TCP`, `Robix.Security.Cryptography`                               |
| `Robix.Network.Proxy`     | `Robix.Network.HTTP`, `Robix.Network.Sockets`                                    |
| `Robix.Network.TLS`       | `Robix.Security.Cryptography`, `Robix.Network.TCP` for stream adapters           |
| `Robix.Network.Email`     | `Robix.Network.TCP`, `Robix.Network.TLS`, `Robix.MIME`                           |
| `Robix.Network.Messaging` | Network transports and message codecs                                            |
| `Robix.Network.RPC`       | `Robix.Network`, `Robix.Serialization`                                           |
| `Robix.Network.gRPC`      | `Robix.Network.HTTP`, Protocol Buffers                                           |
| `Robix.Network.Discovery` | `Robix.Network.DNS`, `Robix.Network.Multicast`                                   |
| `Robix.Network.Multicast` | `Robix.Network.UDP`                                                              |
| `Robix.Network.CAN`       | `Robix.Hardware.CAN`, platform drivers                                           |
| `Robix.Network.QUIC`      | UDP transport, TLS 1.3 cryptographic handshake backend                           |

These relationships are conceptual. They should not introduce circular module dependencies; common connection, certificate, and transport interfaces should live in foundational modules.

## **27. Example: Strictly typed HTTP application**

```
Package Robotics.NetworkClient;


Import Robix.Network.HTTP As HTTP;
Import Robix.JSON As JSON;
Import Robix.Cancellation;
Import Robix.Results;

Public Async Function FetchRobotStatus(
    String url,
    CancellationToken cancellation
) -> Task<Result<RobotStatus, NetworkError>> {

    HTTPClient client = HTTP.CreateClient();

    Try {
        HTTPResponse response = Await client.GetAsync(
            url,
            cancellation
        );

        response.EnsureSuccess();

        RobotStatus status =
            Await response.ReadJSONAsync<RobotStatus>();

        Return Success(status);
    }
    Catch (NetworkError error) {
        Return Failure(error);
    }
    Finally {
        Await client.DisposeAsync();
    }
}
```

This is illustrative Robix syntax. In the finalized implementation, HTTP status failures, JSON parsing failures, cancellation, and disposal behavior should be modeled explicitly in the function's declared error contract.

# **28. Important architectural decisions**

Before freezing these 21 packages, Robix should settle the following:

1. Whether all socket operations use a unified event-loop scheduler.
2. Whether asynchronous methods return `Task<T>` or `Promise<T>` as their canonical public type.
3. Whether networking APIs use exceptions, typed `Result<T,E>`, or both.
4. Whether HTTP/3 and QUIC are bundled by default or require an optional native backend.
5. Whether TLS uses an operating-system cryptographic provider or a separately maintained library.
6. How buffers and streams are owned during asynchronous operations.
7. How cancellation interacts with partial reads, sends, and resource cleanup.
8. Which protocols support automatic reconnection.
9. Whether message framing is part of a common transport interface.
10. How network identity and permissions are enforced for embedded and robotics systems.
11. Which operations are supported on Windows, Linux, macOS, and embedded targets.
12. How to handle API versioning as protocols and security requirements evolve.

## **Final recommendation**

The most maintainable implementation is a layered architecture:

**Application protocols**

HTTP · HTTPS · WebSocket · Email · RPC · gRPC · Messaging · SFTP

**Transport security and services**

TLS · SSH · DNS · Proxy · Discovery

**Transport protocols**

TCP · UDP · QUIC · Multicast · CAN

**Foundational runtime**

Sockets · Streams · Buffers · Async · Cancellation · OS / Hardware backends

## 7. Operating system and system programming

# **ROBIX — Operating System and System Programming API Specification**

21 packages · Classes · Structures · Interfaces · Functions · Async APIs · Native interoperability · System security · Cross-platform support

This is the proposed **Robix Operating System and System Programming Standard Library (ROSSL)**, following the same detailed approach as the previous Core, Mathematics, Algorithms, Filesystem, and Networking specifications.

It covers all **21 packages** you listed, including proposed classes, interfaces, enumerations, functions, platform-specific capabilities, error handling, and implementation rules.

The objective is to make Robix suitable for developing command-line applications, desktop utilities, operating-system tools, service managers, process supervisors, embedded systems, hardware-facing applications, and native integrations.

### **Required design principles**

- **Strict type safety:** All official compiled packages support strict typing.
- **Cross-platform abstractions:** Separate portable system APIs from Windows, Linux, macOS, and other platform backends.
- **Secure permissions:** Privileged operations must have explicit authorization and report permission failures.
- **Async operations:** Process execution, service control, system monitoring, and terminal I/O support asynchronous methods.
- **Resource management:** Native handles, processes, services, and terminal resources support deterministic disposal.
- **Native interoperability:** Foreign types and pointers must have explicit ownership, alignment, lifetime, and calling-convention rules.
- **Predictable errors:** APIs expose typed errors and meaningful platform capability information.
- **No hidden privilege escalation:** System APIs operate only with the calling application's effective permissions.

These are proposed Robix APIs, not claims that the language currently implements every listed feature. Their final signatures will depend on Robix's compiler, runtime, memory model, and supported platforms.

```
Permissions.Set(resource, permissions)
Permissions.Add(resource, permission)
Permissions.Remove(resource, permission)
Permissions.Grant(resource, principal, rights)
Permissions.Revoke(resource, principal, rights)
Permissions.Inherit(resource, parent)
Permissions.Copy(source, destination)
```

**ACL operations**

```
Permissions.GetACL(resource)
Permissions.SetACL(resource, acl)
Permissions.AddACLEntry(acl, entry)
Permissions.RemoveACLEntry(acl, entry)
Permissions.EvaluateACL(acl, principal, action)
Permissions.GetInheritedEntries(acl)
```

**Privilege management**

```
Permissions.GetAvailablePrivileges()
Permissions.GetEnabledPrivileges()
Permissions.CheckPrivilege(privilege)
Permissions.EnablePrivilege(privilege)
Permissions.DisablePrivilege(privilege)
Permissions.DropPrivilege(privilege)
Permissions.DropAllOptionalPrivileges()
```

**Capability management**

```
Permissions.ListCapabilities()
Permissions.HasCapability(capability)
Permissions.RequestCapability(capability)
Permissions.DropCapability(capability)
Permissions.GetCapabilityStatus(capability)
```

**Validation and auditing**

```
Permissions.Validate(permissionSet)
Permissions.Compare(a, b)
Permissions.GetMissing(required, available)
Permissions.Require(permission)
Permissions.AuditAccess(resource, action)
Permissions.GetPermissionDiagnostics()
```

Robix should use **least privilege by default**. A method called `Grant` or `EnablePrivilege` must never bypass the OS security model; it can only perform an authorized operation.

## **7.&#x20;**`Robix.System.Signals`

**Responsibility:** OS signals, process notifications, signal handlers, signal masks, and graceful shutdown coordination.

### **Classes and types**

- `Signal`
- `SignalNumber`
- `SignalName`
- `SignalType`
- `SignalInfo`
- `SignalContext`
- `SignalEvent`
- `SignalHandler`
- `SignalAction`
- `SignalDisposition`
- `SignalMask`
- `SignalSet`
- `SignalManager`
- `SignalSubscription`
- `SignalRegistration`
- `SignalQueue`
- `SignalWaiter`
- `SignalOptions`
- `SignalCapabilities`
- `SignalError`
- `SignalPermissionError`
- `SignalNotSupportedError`
- `GracefulShutdownSignal`

**Common signal constants**

```
SIGINT
SIGTERM
SIGHUP
SIGQUIT
SIGUSR1
SIGUSR2
SIGCHLD
SIGPIPE
SIGALRM
SIGWINCH
SIGSTOP
SIGCONT
```

Signal availability varies by operating system. Some signals cannot be intercepted or ignored.

### **Functions**

**Registration**

```
Signals.Register(signal, handler)
Signals.Unregister(registration)
Signals.Subscribe(signal, handler)
Signals.Unsubscribe(subscription)
Signals.SetHandler(signal, handler)
Signals.ResetHandler(signal)
Signals.Ignore(signal)
Signals.RestoreDefault(signal)
```

**Sending**

```
Signals.Send(processId, signal)
Signals.SendProcessGroup(groupId, signal)
Signals.SendCurrentProcess(signal)
Signals.RequestTermination(processId)
Signals.RequestReload(processId)
```

**Waiting**

```
Signals.Wait(signal)
Signals.WaitAny(signalSet)
Signals.WaitAsync(signal, token)
Signals.WaitAnyAsync(signalSet, token)
Signals.TryWait(signalSet)
Signals.WaitWithTimeout(signalSet, duration)
```

**Signal masks**

```
Signals.Block(signal)
Signals.Unblock(signal)
Signals.SetMask(mask)
Signals.GetMask()
Signals.AddToMask(mask, signal)
Signals.RemoveFromMask(mask, signal)
```

**Inspection**

```
Signals.GetName(signal)
Signals.GetNumber(name)
Signals.GetInfo(signal)
Signals.IsSupported(signal)
Signals.IsCatchable(signal)
Signals.IsBlocked(signal)
Signals.ListSupported()
```

**Graceful shutdown**

```
Signals.OnInterrupt(callback)
Signals.OnTermination(callback)
Signals.OnHangup(callback)
Signals.RegisterShutdownHandler(handler)
Signals.CreateShutdownToken(signals)
Signals.WaitForShutdownAsync()
```

**Critical safety rule:** Low-level signal handlers must not execute arbitrary Robix code directly in an asynchronous signal context. The runtime should convert signals into safely queued events handled by its scheduler or event loop.


**Validation**

```

Commands.CheckAllowedExecutable(command, policy)
Commands.CheckWorkingDirectory(command, policy)
Commands.GetCommandStatistics(command)
```

**Security:** `Run(executable, arguments)` should execute directly without an implicit shell. Shell execution must be an explicit separate API because command strings and argument arrays have fundamentally different injection risks.

## **13.&#x20;**`Robix.System.Registry`

**Responsibility:** Windows Registry operations, registry keys, values, permissions, and change monitoring.

This package should be **Windows-specific**. Other platforms may offer different configuration stores, but they should not pretend to implement Windows Registry semantics.

### **Classes and types**

- `Registry`

```

Registry.LoadHive(path, options)
```

**Platform rules:** Unsupported platforms should return a clear `PlatformNotSupportedError`. Registry virtualization, 32-bit/64-bit views, remote access, and transactions are conditional features.

**Safety:** Native functions must have explicit calling conventions, argument layouts, pointer ownership, and lifetime requirements. Raw system calls should not be presented as portable high-level functionality.

## **15.&#x20;**`Robix.Environment`

**Responsibility:** Robix application execution environment, runtime configuration, application directories, feature flags, deployment environment, and application context.

This differs from `Robix.System.Environment`: the system package manages OS process environment variables; this package provides a coherent **Robix application-level environment**.

### **Classes and types**

- `ApplicationEnvironment`
- `Environment`

**Foreign library management**

```
FFI.LoadLibrary(path)

FFI.ResolveLibrary(name)
```

**Foreign calls**

```
FFI.Call(function, arguments)

```

**Type mapping**

```

```

**Marshalling**

```

FFI.MarshalStruct<T>(value)

```

**Memory ownership**

```
FFI.AllocateForeign(size, allocator)

FFI.CopyFromForeign(pointer, length)

```

**Callbacks**

```
FFI.CreateCallback(delegate, signature)
FFI.RegisterCallback(function, callback)
FFI.GetCallbackPointer(callback)
FFI.ReleaseCallback(callback)
```

**Bindings**

```
FFI.ParseCHeader(path)
FFI.GenerateBindings(header, options)
FFI.GenerateTypeDeclarations(header)
FFI.GenerateFunctionDeclarations(header)
FFI.ValidateBindings(bindings)
FFI.GetBindingDiagnostics(bindings)
```

**ABI inspection**

```
FFI.GetCurrentABI()
FFI.GetCallingConventions()
FFI.IsCallingConventionSupported(convention)
FFI.ValidateFunctionSignature(signature)
FFI.ValidateStructLayout(layout)
```

**Illustrative FFI declaration**

```
Package NativeInteropExample;


Import Robix.FFI;

External Library "native_math" {

    CallingConvention CDecl;

    Public External Function native_add(
        Int32 a,
        Int32 b
    ) -> Int32;
}

Public Function Add(Int32 a, Int32 b) -> Int32 {
    Return native_add(a, b);
}
```

**FFI safety requirements**

- Every imported function must have an explicit ABI and calling convention.
- Pointers must be typed and assigned ownership rules.
- Foreign callbacks must remain alive for the duration required by the foreign library.
- Foreign exceptions must not cross incompatible ABI boundaries.
- Native strings must use a declared encoding.
- Struct padding, alignment, and field offsets must be deterministic.
- Blocking native calls must not unintentionally block the Robix asynchronous scheduler.

## **21.&#x20;**`Robix.Interop`

**Responsibility:** High-level interoperability with other languages, runtimes, applications, components, and data representations.

`Robix.FFI` handles native calling conventions and memory layouts. `Robix.Interop` should provide higher-level adapters for complete foreign objects, runtime environments, modules, and language-specific integrations.

### **Classes and types**

**General interoperability**

- `Interop`
- `InteropManager`
- `InteropContext`
- `InteropOptions`

- `InteropFunction`

- `InteropField`
- `InteropEvent`
- `InteropException`
- `InteropError`
- `InteropResult<T>`

**Type conversion**

- `InteropTypeMapper`

- `InteropObjectMapper`
- `InteropSerializer`
- `InteropMarshaller`
- `InteropNullabilityMapping`
- `InteropCollectionMapping`

- `InteropPromiseAdapter`
- `InteropTaskAdapter`

**Language adapters**

```
Interop.CancelForeignOperation(operation)
```

**Callbacks and events**

```
Interop.CreateCallback(delegate, targetRuntime)
Interop.RegisterEventHandler(object, eventName, handler)
Interop.UnregisterEventHandler(subscription)
Interop.RaiseForeignEvent(object, eventName, arguments)
Interop.CreateDelegateBridge(delegate, targetRuntime)
```

**Module and symbol inspection**

```
Interop.ListModules(runtime)
Interop.ListTypes(module)
Interop.ListFunctions(module)
Interop.GetTypeInfo(type)
Interop.GetMethodInfo(type, name)
Interop.GetModuleMetadata(module)
```

**Data and streams**

```
Interop.ToForeignArray(array, runtime)
Interop.FromForeignArray<T>(value)
Interop.ToForeignStream(stream, runtime)
Interop.FromForeignStream(stream)
Interop.SerializeForRuntime(value, runtime)
Interop.DeserializeFromRuntime<T>(data, runtime)
```

**Diagnostics**

```
Interop.GetLastError()
Interop.GetRuntimeDiagnostics(runtime)
Interop.GetBindingDiagnostics(binding)
Interop.CheckCompatibility(sourceRuntime, targetRuntime)
Interop.GetStatistics()
```

**Design rule:** Foreign runtime integrations may require additional SDK modules and runtime installations. A `PythonInteropProvider`, for example, should not imply that Python is bundled with every Robix installation.

# **22. Shared interfaces for all 21 packages**

The operating-system and system-programming ecosystem should reuse common interfaces wherever possible.

| Interface                    | Responsibility                      |
| ---------------------------- | ----------------------------------- |
| `ISystemInformationProvider` | OS and machine information          |
| `ISystemCapabilities`        | Platform feature detection          |
| `IEnvironmentProvider`       | Environment variable access         |
| `IProcess`                   | Process lifecycle                   |
| `IProcessLauncher`           | Process creation                    |
| `IProcessSupervisor`         | Process supervision                 |
| `IService`                   | OS service abstraction              |
| `IServiceManager`            | OS service control                  |
| `IUserProvider`              | User lookup and account information |
| `IGroupProvider`             | Group management                    |
| `IPermissionProvider`        | Permission checking                 |
| `IAccessControlProvider`     | Access-control management           |
| `ISignalProvider`            | Signal handling                     |
| `ICommandExecutor`           | External command execution          |
| `IPlatformProvider`          | Platform-specific information       |
| `IResourceProvider`          | System resource information         |
| `IResourceLimiter`           | Resource-limit management           |
| `ISystemMonitor`             | System monitoring                   |
| `IMetricProvider`            | Metrics collection                  |
| `IPowerManager`              | Power management                    |
| `IRegistryProvider`          | Windows Registry operations         |
| `INativeHandle`              | Native handle access                |
| `ISafeNativeHandle`          | Owned native resource cleanup       |
| `INativeLibrary`             | Dynamic native-library loading      |
| `IFFIProvider`               | Foreign function calls              |
| `IFFIMarshaller<T>`          | Foreign type marshalling            |
| `IInteropProvider`           | Foreign runtime integration         |
| `IAsyncDisposable`           | Asynchronous disposal               |
| `IDisposable`                | Deterministic disposal              |

## **23. Recommended system error hierarchy**

```
SystemError
    PlatformNotSupportedError
    SystemFeatureUnavailableError
    SystemPermissionError
    SystemResourceError
    SystemOperationFailedError

EnvironmentError
    EnvironmentVariableNotFoundError
    EnvironmentVariableInvalidError
    EnvironmentPermissionError

ProcessError
    ProcessNotFoundError
    ProcessStartError
    ProcessExitError
    ProcessTimeoutError
    ProcessPermissionError
    ProcessResourceLimitError

ServiceError
    ServiceNotFoundError
    ServiceStartError
    ServiceStopError
    ServiceConfigurationError
    ServicePermissionError

UserError
    UserNotFoundError
    GroupNotFoundError
    AccountPermissionError
    AccountValidationError

PermissionError
    PermissionDeniedError
    PrivilegeUnavailableError
    AccessControlError

SignalError
    SignalNotSupportedError
    SignalDeliveryError
    SignalPermissionError

CommandError
    CommandNotFoundError
    CommandExecutionError
    CommandTimeoutError
    CommandValidationError

ResourceError
    ResourceUnavailableError
    ResourceLimitExceededError
    ResourceAllocationError

PowerError
    PowerOperationDeniedError
    PowerFeatureUnavailableError

RegistryError
    RegistryKeyNotFoundError
    RegistryAccessDeniedError
    RegistryTypeMismatchError

NativeError
    NativeLibraryLoadError
    NativeSymbolNotFoundError
    NativeMemoryError
    NativeHandleError

FFIError
    ABICompatibilityError
    FFIMarshallingError
    FFICallbackError
    ForeignCallError

InteropError
    ForeignRuntimeUnavailableError
    ForeignTypeConversionError
    ForeignMethodInvocationError
    ForeignRuntimeException
```

Every error should have a stable `Code`, a descriptive `Message`, optional `Details`, and optional `InnerError`.

## **24. Common API rules**

| Requirement           | Convention                                     |
| --------------------- | ---------------------------------------------- |
| Language typing       | strict typing                            |
| Package naming        | PascalCase, preserving acronyms                |
| Native types          | Explicit sizes and memory layouts              |
| Async methods         | `Async` suffix and `Task<T>`                   |
| Cancellation          | `CancellationToken` for cancellable operations |
| Resource management   | `IDisposable` and `IAsyncDisposable`           |
| Permissions           | Explicit checks and authorization              |
| Error handling        | Typed errors or `Result<T,E>`                  |
| Platform support      | Capability checks                              |
| Command execution     | Explicit argument arrays; no implicit shell    |
| Native pointers       | Unsafe context and lifetime tracking           |
| Service management    | Platform-specific backends                     |
| System monitoring     | Configurable sampling and privacy controls     |
| Privileged operations | Least privilege                                |
| Logging               | Redact sensitive data                          |
| Portability           | Clearly identify unsupported functionality     |

## **25. Recommended ownership and dependencies**

| Package                    | Primary dependencies                            |
| -------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------- |
| `Robix.System`             | `Robix.Core`, `Robix.Runtime`                   |
| `Robix.System.Environment` | `Robix.System`                                  |
| `Robix.System.Processes`   | `Robix.System`, `Robix.IO.Streams`              |
| `Robix.System.Services`    | `Robix.System.Processes`, platform adapters     |
| `Robix.System.Users`       | `Robix.System`, identity backends               |
| `Robix.System.Permissions` | `Robix.System.Users`, security backends         |
| `Robix.System.Signals`     | `Robix.System`, event-loop support              |
| `Robix.System.Commands`    | `Robix.System.Processes`, `Robix.IO`            |
| `Robix.System.Platform`    | `Robix.Runtime`                                 |
| `Robix.System.Resources`   | `Robix.System.Platform`, platform adapters      |
| `Robix.System.Monitoring`  | `Robix.System.Resources`, `Robix.Diagnostics`   |
| `Robix.System.Power`       | `Robix.System.Platform`, OS power backends      |
| `Robix.System.Registry`    | Windows-specific native backend                 |
| `Robix.System.Interop`     | `Robix.Native`, `Robix.FFI`                     |
| `Robix.Environment`        | `Robix.System.Environment`, `Robix.Runtime`     |
| `Robix.CommandLine`        | `Robix.String`, `Robix.Text.Parsing`            |
| `Robix.Console`            | `Robix.IO`, `Robix.Text.Encoding`               |
| `Robix.Terminal`           | `Robix.Console`, native terminal backends       |
| `Robix.Native`             | Platform-specific runtime backend               |
| `Robix.FFI`                | `Robix.Native`, ABI support                     |
| `Robix.Interop`            | `Robix.FFI`, optional language runtime adapters |

These are logical dependencies, not requirements to load every associated package. Shared native abstractions should sit below higher-level OS APIs to prevent circular dependencies.

## **26. Example: Type-safe system information**

```
Package RobixSystemExample;


Import Robix.System As System;
Import Robix.System.Platform As Platform;
Import Robix.System.Resources As Resources;
Import Robix.System.Processes As Processes;
Import Robix.Console As Console;

Public Function Main() -> ExitCode {

    String osName = System.GetOSName();

    Int32 cpuCount = Resources.GetCPUCount();

    Int64 totalMemory = Resources.GetTotalMemory();

    Process current = Processes.GetCurrentProcess();

    Console.WriteLine("Operating System: " + osName);
    Console.WriteLine("CPU Count: " + cpuCount);
    Console.WriteLine("Total Memory: " + totalMemory);
    Console.WriteLine("Process: " + current.Name);

    Return ExitCode.SUCCESS;
}
```

## **27. Example: Secure asynchronous process execution**

A Robix application should be able to launch a process without invoking a shell implicitly.

```
Package RobixProcessExample;


Import Robix.System.Processes As Processes;
Import Robix.Results;

Public Async Function ExecuteTool(
    String executable,
    Array<String> arguments,
    CancellationToken cancellation
) -> Task<Result<ProcessResult, ProcessError>> {

    ProcessStartInfo startInfo = ProcessStartInfo {
        Executable: executable,
        Arguments: arguments,
        RedirectStandardOutput: True,
        RedirectStandardError: True,
        UseShell: False
    };

    Return Await Processes.TryRunAsync(
        startInfo,
        cancellation
    );
}
```

This example assumes a `TryRunAsync` convenience API that should be added to the finalized process package. Its implementation must drain stdout and stderr without deadlocking, enforce output limits, and handle cancellation and process-tree cleanup.

## **28. Important architecture decisions**

Before making these APIs permanent, the Robix project should decide:

1. Which operating systems Robix officially supports.
2. Whether native compilation, bytecode, or both are used.
3. Whether `Unsafe` is a language keyword or a compiler-recognized context.
4. How native memory ownership is tracked.
5. Whether process and service APIs use OS-native handles or abstract handles.
6. Which platform features require optional runtime modules.
7. Whether Robix supports hosting multiple foreign runtimes inside one process.
8. How native callbacks interact with Robix garbage collection.
9. How native functions report errors and propagate exceptions.
10. Whether the CLI and terminal APIs are bundled by default.
11. How process cancellation handles child processes and partial output.
12. Whether resource limits are best-effort or strongly enforced.
13. Which permissions can be inspected versus modified.
14. How native library compatibility and ABI versions are verified.
15. Whether the runtime supports sandboxed execution of system operations.

## **Final architecture recommendation**

**Application-facing APIs**

Environment · CommandLine · Console · Terminal

**Portable system services**

Processes · Services · Users · Permissions · Signals · Resources · Monitoring · Power

**Native interoperability**

System.Interop · Interop · FFI · Native

**Operating-system backends**

Windows · Linux · macOS · BSD · Mobile · Embedded

The key distinction should be between **portable system functionality** and **platform-dependent native operations**. Robix applications should use portable APIs whenever possible, while native APIs should remain available for systems programming with explicit safety boundaries.

## 9. Security and cryptography

| Package | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `Robix.Security` | General security functionality |
| `Robix.Security.Cryptography` | Cryptographic primitives and APIs |
| `Robix.Security.Hashing` | Cryptographic and non-cryptographic hash APIs |
| `Robix.Security.Encryption` | Encryption and decryption APIs |
| `Robix.Security.Signatures` | Digital signatures and verification |
| `Robix.Security.KeyManagement` | Key generation, storage, and lifecycle |
| `Robix.Security.Certificates` | Certificate parsing and validation |
| `Robix.Security.TLS` | TLS certificate and policy helpers |
| `Robix.Security.Authentication` | Authentication |
| `Robix.Security.Authorization` | Authorization |
| `Robix.Security.Tokens` | Security token handling |
| `Robix.Security.Passwords` | Password hashing and verification |
| `Robix.Security.Random` | Cryptographically secure randomness |
| `Robix.Security.Secrets` | Secret management |
| `Robix.Security.Permissions` | Access-control management |

### 9.1 Cryptographic algorithms and primitives

These are algorithm APIs or implementation families, not necessarily separate top-level packages. They should be grouped under the security namespaces above.

| Algorithm family | Algorithms / standards to consider |
|---|---|
| Hashing | SHA-2 family (`SHA-256`, `SHA-384`, `SHA-512`), SHA-3 family, SHAKE |
| Password hashing | Argon2id, scrypt, bcrypt, PBKDF2 |
| Message authentication | HMAC, KMAC, Poly1305 |
| Authenticated encryption | AES-GCM, AES-CCM, ChaCha20-Poly1305 |
| Symmetric encryption | AES |
| Public-key encryption / key establishment | RSA-OAEP, ECDH, X25519, ML-KEM |
| Digital signatures | RSA-PSS, ECDSA, Ed25519, ML-DSA, SLH-DSA |
| Key derivation | HKDF, PBKDF2, scrypt, Argon2id |
| Checksums / error detection | CRC-32, CRC-64, Adler-32 |
| Encoding (not encryption) | Base16, Base32, Base64, Base64URL, hexadecimal |
| Secure transport | TLS 1.2 and TLS 1.3; certificate validation |
| Randomness | Operating-system CSPRNG and secure random APIs |

**Security policy:** Robix should prefer modern, reviewed algorithms and safe high-level APIs. Deprecated or unsafe algorithms should be available only through explicitly marked compatibility APIs, with warnings. For example, MD5 and SHA-1 must not be recommended for security-sensitive hashing; ECB mode should not be offered as a normal encryption default; and new protocols should not invent their own cryptographic constructions. Algorithm availability must follow the target platform's validated implementation and applicable security requirements.

## 19. Additional algorithm catalogue

The following well-known algorithms and algorithm families should be considered for the corresponding packages. This is an API coverage roadmap, not a requirement to expose every algorithm as an individual package.

### 19.1 Sorting
- Bubble sort
- Selection sort
- Insertion sort
- Shell sort
- Merge sort
- Quicksort
- Heapsort
- Introsort
- TimSort
- Counting sort
- Radix sort
- Bucket sort
- External merge sort
- Topological ordering

### 19.2 Searching
- Linear search
- Binary search
- Jump search
- Interpolation search
- Exponential search
- Ternary search
- Hash-based lookup
- Trie lookup
- Range queries

### 19.3 Graph algorithms
- Breadth-first search (BFS)
- Depth-first search (DFS)
- Dijkstra's algorithm
- Bellman-Ford algorithm
- Floyd-Warshall algorithm
- A* search
- Johnson's algorithm
- Prim's algorithm
- Kruskal's algorithm
- Topological sort
- Tarjan's strongly connected components algorithm
- Kosaraju's algorithm
- Edmonds-Karp maximum flow
- Dinic's maximum flow
- Union-Find / Disjoint Set Union

### 19.4 String algorithms
- Knuth-Morris-Pratt (KMP)
- Boyer-Moore
- Rabin-Karp
- Z algorithm
- Aho-Corasick
- Levenshtein distance
- Damerau-Levenshtein distance
- Longest common subsequence
- Longest common substring
- Manacher's algorithm
- Suffix arrays and suffix trees
- Trie and prefix matching

### 19.5 Numerical algorithms
- Euclidean and extended Euclidean algorithms
- Fast exponentiation
- Newton-Raphson method
- Bisection method
- Secant method
- Gaussian elimination
- LU decomposition
- QR decomposition
- Cholesky decomposition
- Singular value decomposition
- Fast Fourier Transform (FFT)
- Discrete Fourier Transform (DFT)
- Numerical integration
- Numerical differentiation
- Interpolation
- Root finding

### 19.6 Compression and coding
- Huffman coding
- Arithmetic coding
- LZ77
- LZ78
- LZW
- DEFLATE
- Run-length encoding
- Burrows-Wheeler transform
- Delta encoding
- Prefix coding

### 19.7 Cryptographic algorithms
- AES
- RSA
- SHA-256
- SHA-512
- SHA-3
- HMAC
- HKDF
- PBKDF2
- Argon2id
- scrypt
- bcrypt
- ChaCha20-Poly1305
- Ed25519
- ECDSA
- X25519
- ML-KEM
- ML-DSA
- SLH-DSA

Cryptographic APIs must follow current security guidance, use secure randomness, and avoid exposing unsafe primitives as defaults.

### 19.8 Machine-learning algorithms
- Linear regression
- Logistic regression
- Decision trees
- Random forests
- Gradient boosting

- Stochastic gradient descent

- Q-learning

### 19.9 Scheduling and optimization
- First Come, First Served (FCFS)
- Shortest Job First (SJF)
- Round Robin
- Priority scheduling
- Earliest Deadline First (EDF)
- A* optimization/search
- Branch and bound
- Dynamic programming optimization
- Genetic algorithms
- Simulated annealing
- Tabu search
- Constraint satisfaction

### 19.10 Geometry and spatial algorithms
- Convex hull (Graham scan, monotone chain)
- Line intersection
- Point-in-polygon
- Closest pair of points
- Sweep-line algorithms
- R-tree spatial indexing
- k-d trees
- Voronoi diagrams
- Delaunay triangulation

## 20. Package-system rules

1. **Acronym capitalization:** Use conventional uppercase acronyms in package identifiers, such as `Robix.Network.TLS`, `Robix.Network.SSH`, `Robix.Network.HTTP`, and `Robix.Database.SQL`.
2. **Consistent namespaces:** Keep related functionality under a common parent namespace.
3. **Core availability:** Core packages ship with every Robix installation.
4. **Standard availability:** Standard packages ship with the SDK/runtime distribution and are imported when needed.
5. **Specialized availability:** Platform-dependent packages may require an SDK component, native backend, driver, or supported device.
6. **Strict typing:** Every official package must support strict typing.
7. **Async APIs:** I/O-heavy packages should offer asynchronous APIs where appropriate.
8. **Documentation:** Every public type and member must have API documentation.
9. **Versioning:** Packages declare compatible Robix language, runtime, and API versions.
10. **Security:** Security-sensitive APIs use safe defaults and clearly mark deprecated or unsafe operations.
11. **Modular compilation:** Unused packages should be excluded from an application where the compiler and linker can safely do so.
12. **No false availability promise:** A package namespace may be part of the SDK while a particular provider or hardware backend is unavailable on a target platform.

## 21. Example imports

```robix
Package RobotController;


Import Robix.Core;
Import Robix.Math As Math;
Import Robix.Network.HTTP As HTTP;
Import Robix.Network.TLS As TLS;
Import Robix.Network.SSH As SSH;
Import Robix.JSON As JSON;
Import Robix.Algorithms.Sorting As Sorting;
Import Robix.Security.Cryptography As Crypto;
Import Robix.Hardware.GPIO As GPIO;
Import Robix.Robotics.Navigation As Navigation;
Import Robix.AI.Inference As AI;

Public Async Function Main() -> Task<ExitCode> {
    Float distance = Math.Sqrt(144);

    Array<Int> values = [9, 2, 7, 1];
    Sorting.QuickSort(values);

    TLS.Client client = TLS.Client.CreateDefault();
    HTTP.Response response = Await HTTP.Get(
        "https://example.com",
        tls: client
    );

    Print(distance);
    Print(values);
    Print(response.StatusCode);

    Return ExitCode.SUCCESS;
}
```

## 22. Important implementation distinction

The catalogue defines package names and intended responsibilities. It does not mean that every protocol, cryptographic algorithm, database provider, codec, AI model, or hardware driver must be implemented inside the Robix compiler itself.

A clean implementation should separate:

- **Language/compiler:** syntax, type checking, generics, async semantics, and code generation.
- **Core runtime:** memory, scheduling, exceptions, Tasks, and fundamental types.
- **Standard library:** portable APIs and algorithms.
- **Platform backends:** operating-system and hardware-specific implementations.
- **Optional SDK components:** specialized providers, codecs, drivers, and accelerators.

This separation keeps the Robix compiler manageable while allowing the built-in ecosystem to grow.

## 38. Package design rules for the expanded catalogue

1. **Do not promise every package as a built-in implementation.** Classify each as Core, Standard, Optional SDK, or Platform Adapter.
2. **Keep the compiler small.** The compiler should only require packages needed for language semantics, type checking, code generation, and runtime support.
3. **Preserve strict typing.** Every official package must publish typed signatures, generic constraints, nullability rules, and error behavior compatible with strict typing.
4. **Provide asynchronous APIs where useful.** Network, filesystem, database, cloud, messaging, and other I/O-bound APIs should provide `Async` variants and support cancellation.
5. **Use capability checks.** Hardware, native OS, GPU, camera, audio, and platform-specific features must expose availability checks rather than assuming a device exists.
6. **Avoid namespace collisions.** A package should have one canonical fully qualified name. Use aliases at import sites when shorter names are useful.
7. **Separate interfaces from providers.** For example, `Robix.Cloud.Storage` defines common abstractions while provider adapters live in separately versioned packages.
8. **Version packages independently where appropriate.** Define compatibility rules for package APIs, compiler versions, runtime versions, and target platforms.
9. **Secure defaults are mandatory.** Cryptography, networking, identity, serialization, and file-processing packages should use safe defaults and document unsafe escape hatches.
10. **Document availability and dependencies.** Each package should state its target platforms, native dependencies, optional drivers, licensing constraints, and support status.

## 39. Suggested package maturity levels

| Level | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `Core` | Required for the language/runtime to operate |
| `Standard` | Official package shipped with the normal SDK |
| `Optional` | Official package installed only when requested |
| `Platform` | Official adapter dependent on an operating system or device |
| `Experimental` | Preview API that may change before stabilization |
| `ThirdParty` | Independently maintained package, not guaranteed by the Robix language project |

64. New package naming and architecture recommendations
For a language as extensive as Robix, I recommend introducing a formal package classification system:
Classification	Meaning
Core	Required language/runtime functionality
Standard	Official general-purpose libraries
Advanced	Specialized official libraries
Platform	OS-specific or hardware-specific libraries
Scientific	Scientific and engineering libraries
Experimental	APIs still under development
ThirdParty	Community-maintained packages
